<summary>This title page introduces a mystical treatise by Johann Georg Gichtel and Johann Georg Graber concerning the "Three Principles" of existence and how they manifest as spiritual centers within the human body. The work aims to help readers identify which spiritual forces govern their lives and provides guidance on spiritual warfare and true prayer.</summary>

<summary>This title page introduces a mystical and theosophical guide to the "inner man," focusing on the spiritual centers of the body and the struggle for divine alignment. It highlights the work of Johann Georg Gichtel, a prominent 17th-century mystic known for his visual representations of the soul's anatomy.</summary>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page serves as the preface to a new edition of mystical writings, establishing continuity by explaining the long delay in publishing the accompanying illustrations.</meta>

header: ❦ * 159

---

## Preface.

->I.<-

The beloved reader receives here, contrary to all expectation, that which he has hoped for over so many years. These are the **Figures of the Inner Man** <note>These are likely mystical or emblematic diagrams representing the spiritual state of the human soul, common in 17th-century Pietist and Theosophical literature.</note>, which our author kept hidden with him for 15 years until the end of his life, and which we have held for another 10 years after his passing;

2. They were intended to remain secret even longer, had Love <note>The author likely refers to "Love" as a personification of Divine Wisdom or Sophia.</note> not moved anew in the spirit for the sake of her lovers, and finally given permission and strengthened us to entrust them to the press, and to add them to the general new printing of this blessed and expanded third edition of the *Anointed Letters* <note>original: "gesalbten Briefe"; likely referring to the collected epistles of a mystical author like Jacob Böhme or Johann Georg Gichtel</note>, as a lovely ornament; serving at the same time as a key for the reader, within the spirit of understanding, to better comprehend what he reads.

3. We rejoice with you over **God’s** great kindness with a childlike heart—we who wish one another well and praise **God** together for it.

4. Receive then this gift, which Love has bestowed just as the printing was already very far advanced, with a modest heart as if from **God**, and use it, like all the author's writings, for the salvation and benefit of your soul.

<meta>catchword: 5.</meta>

<summary>This preface introduces the "Figures of the Inner Man," mystical diagrams published here for the first time after being kept secret for twenty-five years. The publishers present these illustrations as a spiritual "key" to help readers better understand the author's collected letters.</summary>
<keywords>Figures of the Inner Man, Anointed Letters, third edition, spiritual diagrams, mysticism, preface, Divine Love, soul's salvation</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface, shifting from the history of the "Figures" (illustrations) to the spiritual inspiration behind their inclusion and the publisher's role in completing the deceased author's work.</meta>

<page-num>II</page-num>
<header>Preface.</header>

5. The dear reader will also find it pleasing that we have provided a brief introduction to the figures which we have incorporated into this writing at every relevant place. We were moved to do so and set upon it in our hearts when **God’s** Spirit opened itself and gave us the word to fulfill this desire:

6. Also, in a certain sense, to fulfill what did not wish to flow from the Author’s own pen; for although one cannot express the divine riches all at once, one can speak only as much as the Holy Spirit—who holds the key to them—grants to be spoken at each time.

7. In the outflow of that same grace, we have then also gone through the **Process of Jesus** <term>original: "Proceß JEsu"; a mystical concept referring to the inner transformation of the soul through the imitation of the stages of Christ's life, from birth to resurrection.</term>, considering what our dearest Savior has done in our humanity since then, and how it has been further advanced in the spirit until this time, through the power of **Jesus**, who is the author and finisher of our faith.

8. Praise **God**! that we have lived to see the day for which the Author so longingly yearned in his own time. However, the labor of birth <note>original: "Geburtsarbeit"; refers to the difficult spiritual and intellectual struggle of bringing these mystical truths and illustrations into the physical world.</note> involving the outer spirit (which bore down powerfully upon the outer man) exceeded the natural strength of old age; therefore, the Virgin <note>referring to Divine Wisdom, or Sophia, a central figure in this mystical tradition who acts as the spiritual bride of the soul.</note> called her Bridegroom to herself and laid this strict and hard labor upon those of a younger age.

9. In this, we also had **Jesus** with us, who allowed us to enjoy the fruits of our champion's <note>original: "Streiters"; literally "fighter" or "warrior," a common term for a person engaged in intense spiritual struggle or "theopathy."</note> youthful labors for the common benefit of all fellow-strugglers. To the praise of **God**, Amen.

---

<summary>The publishers explain that they have added introductions to the mystical illustrations, completing work the author was unable to finish before his death. They frame the publication as a spiritual labor assisted by the Holy Spirit and dedicated to the "fellow-strugglers" in the faith.</summary>
<keywords>Holy Spirit, Process of Jesus, Sophia, Virgin, Bridegroom, spiritual labor, divine riches, fellow-strugglers</keywords>

<page-num>III</page-num>
<page-type>preface</page-type>

<image-desc>Three decorative ornaments arranged horizontally at the top of the page.</image-desc>

## Preface

### To the God- and Wisdom-loving Reader.

->I.<-

1. Although I have been very hesitant <note>original: "blöd"; in this period, the word meant shy, timid, or reluctant rather than its modern meaning of "stupid."</note> and cautious about sharing this little paradise flower <term>original: "Paradisblümlein"; a common mystical metaphor for a fragile but beautiful divine revelation or spiritual insight sprouted within the soul.</term>—which was revealed to me through my great and earnest struggle—with this present coarse and swinish world, which tramples the noble pearl of the knowledge of **God** and oneself underfoot and tears those who love it to pieces; yet my dear fellow-seekers <note>original: "Mitstreiter"; literally "fellow combatants," referring to those engaged in the same spiritual struggle.</note> have overcome me through their manifold urging to bring this to light as a guide for those who search for the Mystery of God <note>original: "Myſterio GOttes"</note> in their innermost depths, so that they may have a mirror before their eyes in which to behold themselves.

2. It is also intended as a reminder and a warning, because Adam missed his chance <note>original: "die Schanze versehen"; an idiomatic expression meaning to lose an advantage, fail in a duty, or make a fatal mistake, referring here to the Fall of Man.</note> and awakened the cold, fierce darkness within his life-forms <term>original: "Lebensgestalten"; in theosophical writing, these are the fundamental qualities or "forms" that constitute a being's existence.</term> and made it active, thereby awakening in all his branches and children a violent struggle of evil against good, of darkness, wrath, and the "No" against the light, love, and the "Yes."

<sig>* 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: 3.</meta>

<summary>The author opens his preface by explaining that he was initially reluctant to publish his spiritual revelations for a "swinish world," but did so at the urging of fellow seekers to provide a mirror for self-reflection and a warning about Adam's fall.</summary>
<keywords>preface, wisdom, pearl of great price, paradise flower, self-knowledge, Adam, the Fall, spiritual struggle, the Yes and No</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's preface from page III, moving from the initial motivation for writing the book to specific spiritual warnings for the reader. The author emphasizes the constant state of spiritual warfare within the human soul.</meta>

<page-num>IV</page-num>
<header>IV Preface.</header>

3. Therefore, every person must be on their guard, diligently and earnestly watching and praying, and must also carefully keep a sharp watch over all the evil, poisonous influences of the hellish and earthly constellations <note>original: "Gestirns"; in this context, it refers not just to stars, but to the spiritual and physical atmospheres or "currents" that influence human behavior.</note>. One must pay close attention to the pointer on the scale <term>original: "Waagzünglein"; literally the "little tongue" of a balance scale, representing the delicate tipping point of the will.</term>, or the desire, lust, and ***Imagination*** <note>In 17th-century mystical thought, "Imagination" was the power of the mind to "form" or "clothe" itself in either spiritual or worldly desires.</note> of the mind <term>original: "Gemüth"; a word encompassing the heart, soul, and disposition.</term>, so that the Good is not outweighed, and one’s fall and rift do not become even greater.

4. This is very easily and quickly overlooked when the soul becomes a little too self-assured and careless, incautiously turning itself toward earthly things and allowing itself to be made "pregnant" by the spirit of this world through earthly lust, or by allowing a fierce thought against a brother to enter, or by rising up in pride <note>original: "Hoffart"</note> above the thrones <note>A reference to the fall of Lucifer, who sought to exalt himself above his divinely appointed station.</note>.

5. For all three **Principles** <note>original: "Principia"; in theosophical tradition, these represent the three realms of existence: the dark fire-world, the light-world, and the outward material world.</note> hunger for the soul: each seeks the supreme rule and struggles for it until it has either conquered or has been conquered and bound into subjection by the strongest—namely, by the Light, which alone is almighty. I have experienced this well in my own long-standing, earnest struggle <note>original: "Kampf"</note>.

6. And it is not enough that many begin with a good intention and earnestness, and even persevere in it for eight, nine, ten or more years, only to finally give way through frivolity, harming not only themselves, but also

<meta>catchword: others</meta>

<summary>The author warns the reader to maintain constant spiritual vigilance, explaining that the soul is a battlefield where three competing principles—darkness, light, and the world—struggle for mastery.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual warfare, Three Principles, vigilance, imagination, pride, the Fall, inner struggle</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface from page IV, concluding a list of spiritual warnings and requirements for the believer. It focuses on the necessity of total surrender to God and the dangers of spiritual arrogance or lack of preparation.</meta>

<page-num>V</page-num>
<header>Preface. V</header>

...offends other weak hearts, and plunges them into eternal ruin along with himself;

7. Instead, he must sacrifice his entire life to the Lord, eternally surrendering himself—body, soul, and spirit, as well as his goods and blood—and consider that he has been called to rebirth <term>original: "Wiedergeburt"; a central concept in Christian mysticism referring to a spiritual awakening and the total transformation of the inner person.</term> by God out of pure grace. He has promised eternal loyalty and steadfastness to his God, and is bound to stand firm with Christ as his head, and to lay down his life for his testimony of the truth.

8. And what does it help a person if he loves his earthly life and seeks to preserve it? It is, after all, fragile, and according to Christ’s word, he will eventually lose it. It is much better that one does not put his hand to the plow <note>A reference to Luke 9:62: "No one who puts a hand to the plow and looks back is fit for service in the kingdom of God."</note> and, in his simplicity, waits patiently for the Lord’s call; or, if he is indeed driven in his mind <term>original: "Gemüth"; a term encompassing the heart, soul, and disposition.</term> by God’s Spirit, that he walks in humility. He should often calculate the sum of his inward strength, whether he dares to stand with ten thousand against twenty thousand <note>A reference to the parable in Luke 14:31-32 about "calculating the cost" of following Christ.</note>, and rather walk slowly than fly, despise his enemy, and finally be overcome.

9. Which I write as a warning to those who indeed joyfully enter into the following of the Lord, but as yet for no ear-

<sig>* 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: -nest (completing the word "earnest" / "ernsten")</meta>

<summary>This page outlines the total commitment required of a spiritual seeker, emphasizing the need for humility and a realistic assessment of one's inner strength. It warns against starting the spiritual journey prematurely or arrogantly, using the biblical metaphor of calculating the costs of war.</summary>
<keywords>rebirth, steadfastness, spiritual warfare, cost of discipleship, humility, Gemüth, martyrdom</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the preface from page V. The opening text completes the word "earnest" (German: "ernst-") from the previous page's final sentence, describing the "earnest battle" the soul must face.</meta>

<page-num>VI</page-num>

<header>Preface.</header>

-nest battle: For the beginning is commonly sweet, joyful, and very pleasant; but when it comes to the earnestness of the matter—where the soul must withdraw its will from the outer **stars** <term>original: "Gestirne"; refers to the astrological and cosmic influences that Böhme believed governed the physical body and the "outer" worldly mind.</term>, and turn itself into God into her **Center** <term>original: "Centrum"; the inward point of the soul where it meets the divine presence.</term>, leaving all visible things behind, and must penetrate through the **eighth form of fire** <term>original: "achte Gestalt des Feuers"; in Böhmean theosophy, the eighth form represents a transitional state or a "dividing point" between the dark world of nature and the light world of God's majesty.</term>—then it costs labor and bloody sweat, for the soul must wrestle with God and men.

10. Now, if you wish to love your life and look back <note>An allusion to the biblical warning that "no one who puts his hand to the plow and looks back is fit for the kingdom of God" (Luke 9:62).</note>, then the devil comes with seven spirits more evil than himself <note>A reference to Matthew 12:45.</note>, locking up your poor soul in all its forms, so that for the entire duration of your life you remain a slave to the world-spirit in great misery, poverty, hunger, and earthly **belly-care** <term>original: "Bauchsorge"; obsessive anxiety over material needs and physical survival.</term>, and a prisoner of the devil; and finally, you must enter into the fire of God with trembling, anguish, and woe. Of this I have, alas, witnessed sorrowful examples in my time.

11. In this time, you are your own maker: If you remain in humility and make an angel of yourself, then you are an angel; but if you make a proud, flying devil of yourself, then you are a devil, and you cannot blame God.

12. The fire of the eighth form is the **dividing goal** <term>original: "Scheideziel"; a point of separation or a final destination where the soul is tested and sorted.</term>: which figure I indeed from

<meta>catchword: Böhme's</meta>

<summary>This page warns the reader of the intense spiritual struggle required to achieve rebirth, emphasizing that the soul must choose between divine humility and worldly attachment. It introduces the mystical concept of the "eighth form of fire" as the ultimate point of spiritual transformation and judgment.</summary>

<keywords>spiritual battle, stars, eighth form of fire, center of the soul, belly-care, devil, angel, humility, free will, Jacob Böhme</keywords>

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s preface from page VI. The text transitions from the warning about spiritual failure to explaining the purpose of the illustrations (figures) included in this work, which are derived from the mystical philosophy of Jakob Böhme.</meta>

<page-num>VII</page-num>
<header>Preface.</header>

[...continued from previous page] borrowed from Böhme’s <note>Jakob Böhme (1575–1624), a German Lutheran mystic whose complex theories on the nature of God and the soul heavily influenced later Pietism and Western esotericism.</note> writing on the **threefold life** <term>original: "dreyfachen Leben"; refers to Böhme's work *The Threefold Life of Man*, which describes human existence as a tension between the dark world of fire, the light world of God, and the outer physical world.</term>, but for the sake of simplicity placed into such **figures** <note>In this context, "figures" refers to the symbolic diagrams or illustrations that often accompanied Böhme's dense theological texts to make them more accessible.</note>, because man has become so very earthly and externalized that he is always speculating about what is above him, seeking that which is very near to him inwardly in the **Center of the soul** <term>original: "Centro der Seele"; the mystical "point" where the human spirit meets the divine presence.</term> far above the starry firmament in the heights of eternity.

13. Also, in the following figures it is painted before your eyes how the **three worlds** are within one another, or stand beneath one another, just as the gracious God has revealed them to me, even leading my spirit into all the Centers. For I only indicate the Centers: If anyone wishes to make large circles or **globes** <note>original: "Globos" (Latin: globes/spheres).</note> and draw them into one another, they may do so; as I have seen it in the spirit, so have I recorded it.

14. And although not everyone attains to **visual clarity** <term>original: "Schaulichkeit"; the spiritual gift of "seeing" or perceiving divine truths as clear, intuitive visions rather than just intellectual concepts.</term>—for it depends upon divine grace and steadfast faithfulness, victory and overcoming—yet an earnest warrior will become well aware of these things within himself through his own feeling and taste, if he walks intimately with God.

15. In addition to this, I [describe] in the fifth chapter the battle of **Michael** <note>original: "Michaelis" (Latin genitive).</note> and the **dragon** <note>A reference to Revelation 12:7, interpreted here as the internal spiritual struggle between the divine spirit and the ego or "serpent" nature within the human soul.</note>, what it is, and how it [occurs] in the creature through sep-

<meta>catchword: sep- (German: Tren-)</meta>

<summary>The author explains that the book's illustrations are simplified versions of Jakob Böhme's complex ideas, designed to help "earthly" people find God within their own souls rather than in the distant sky. He asserts that while not everyone will have mystical visions, those who walk with God will experience these spiritual truths internally.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Threefold Life, Three Worlds, Center of the Soul, spiritual combat, Michael and the Dragon, visual clarity, mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 22 ---
<meta>This page concludes the author’s preface, completing the summary of the book's chapters and offering a final blessing to the reader. It continues the discussion of the soul’s spiritual journey toward union with the divine.</meta>

<page-num>VIII</page-num>
# ->Preface.<-

The separation and division of the **One Will** <term>original: "Einen Willens"; in Böhmean thought, the unified divine will that becomes fragmented into many selfish, "contrary" wills through the Fall of man.</term> into many contrary wills arises, and also what **temptations** befall the **Son of the Virgin** <note>original: "Jungfrauensohn"; a mystical term for the "new man" or the Christ-like spirit born within the believer through the influence of Divine Wisdom (Sophia).</note> before he is fully **sealed** in the **spiritual marriage** <term>original: "geistlichen Ehe"; the *unio mystica* or the final, permanent union between the human soul and the Divine.</term>, is added and discussed.

16. And finally, in the sixth chapter, something is reported concerning **prayer and its mystery** <note>original: "Mysterio" (Latin-derived: mystery).</note>, namely, what it is to pray rightly in **spirit and truth** <note>A reference to the Gospel of John 4:24: "God is spirit, and his worshipers must worship in the Spirit and in truth."</note>, as I have learned it from my own experience, because it is a **warrior’s** <term>original: "Streiters"; the spiritual combatant or "soldier of Christ" who undergoes the inner struggle for sanctification.</term> indispensable sword.

17. May the dear reader receive it in love, use it for his benefit, and thank **God**, from whom I received it. Into whose precious love I also commend the reader, and I commend myself to your Christian prayer.

---

# The Image of God in Man,
## The Divine Essence.

<image-desc>At the top left, a portion of a circular astrological or cosmological diagram is visible, featuring a wavy Aquarius-like symbol, a star, the number 30, and the letter M. A large rectangular paper flap is pasted over the center of the page, through which faint outlines of the underlying circular diagram—including stars, radial lines, and numbers—can be seen.</image-desc>

3. Especially as he intends here, as an introduction, only to show you in general what the **originally created image of God** was before the **Fall** <note>Referring to the biblical Fall of Adam, which in this mystical context represents the loss of man's original divine nature.</note>, which exists in the **hiddenness of the spirit**. This was recognized by the author in practice <note>original: "in der Praxi" (Latin); referring to experiential knowledge gained through spiritual exercise rather than just theory.</note>, and has been unfolded for you in the figures <note>original: "Figuren"; the symbolic illustrations or diagrams found throughout the book.</note> of the **perfect man**, arising from the **new birth in Christ** <note>original: "in Christo" (Latin).</note>.

4. And within you, through **rebirth**, this will also gradually come to its essence <term>original: "Wesen"; the true spiritual substance or fundamental being of a person.</term> and be recognized, if you follow the writer in earnest.

<vocab>Image of God, originally created, hiddenness, author, practice, figures, new birth, Christ, rebirth, essence</vocab>

<summary>This page introduces the spiritual goal of the book: recovering the original, pre-Fall image of God within the human soul through the process of rebirth and practical spiritual experience.</summary>
<keywords>Image of God, the Fall, spiritual rebirth, new birth in Christ, divine essence, mystical practice</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the preface (Vorrede) of the work. VIII

### Preface

...the separation and division of the **One Will** into many contrary wills arose, and also what temptations befall the **Virgin-Son** <term>original: "Jungfrauensohn"; a mystical term for the spiritual "new man" born within the soul through Divine Wisdom.</term> before he is completely sealed in the **spiritual marriage**, are added and discussed.

16. And finally, in the sixth chapter, I have reported something concerning **prayer** and its **mystery** <note>original: "Myſterio" (Latin/Greek root); here referring to the hidden spiritual reality or inner working of prayer.</note>, specifically what it means to pray rightly in spirit and truth, as I have learned from my own experience, because it is an indispensable **sword** for a spiritual warrior.

17. May the dear reader receive it in love, use it for his benefit, and thank **God**, from whom I have received it. To whose precious love I also commend the reader, and I commend myself to his Christian prayer.

---

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the author's preface, outlining the final chapters on the conflict of the human will and the vital role of prayer as a spiritual weapon. The author ends with a humble dedication, commending the reader to God's love and requesting their prayers.</summary>

# The Image of God in Man,
## Of Divine Substance.

# Chapter One.

## Introduction.

->1.<-

God-seeking reader! Here, the <term>Wheel of Birth<note>A central concept in German theosophy (associated with Jacob Böhme), referring to the eternal cycle of nature's forces and the process by which divine life manifests.</note></term> according to all three principles is shared with you, undivided and whole, so that you may see in advance how the forms or powers in the "new man" <note>The "new man" refers to the spiritually reborn self in Christian mysticism.</note> are born in their proper order within the <term>mind<note>German: *Gemüth*; this term refers to the deep inner seat of a person's emotions, will, and soul, rather than just the intellect.</note></term>.

2. Imagine this vividly within your mind, and you will learn to understand our author in the following illustrations all the more easily, because understanding resides deep within the mind.

3. Especially since he wishes to show you here, as an introduction, what the first-created Image of God was like before the Fall—which remains hidden within the spirit, was recognized by the author through <term>practice<note>original: *Praxi*; here referring to spiritual experience or lived devotion.</note></term>, and has been unfolded for you in the figures of the perfected man through the new birth in Christ.

4. And in you, through <term>rebirth<note>original: *Widergeburt*; the process of spiritual regeneration.</note></term>, it will gradually come into being and be recognized, if you follow the writer in earnest.

# Of the Great Mystery

## Of the Great Mystery of Divine Revelation, according to Time and Eternity in the Human Image.

->I.<-

When we wish to contemplate and consider man in his inner, deep birth, we must simply turn our soul away from the earthly, astral, and elemental life, and toward the inner Divine life of Jesus Christ within us. We must call upon this dear Physician for grace, that He might graciously reopen the eyes of those of us born blind by nature—the eyes that the devil closed in Paradise—so that we may regain our lost <term>eye of light<note>German: *Lichts-Auge*; refers to spiritual intuition or the ability to see divine truth directly, as opposed to natural or intellectual reasoning.</note></term> and be able to recognize and behold God within us. Otherwise, this will surely remain a sealed mystery and be incomprehensible to our rational <term>astral eye<note>German: *Stern-Auge*; the worldly or natural intellect which, according to this philosophy, is influenced by the physical universe (the "stars") but cannot perceive eternal, spiritual truths.</note></term>.

2. For what we see externally in man and his conduct—aside from language and the natural governance of sensory reason—is common to all animals. And when man reaches the end of his time of birth, he returns to his mother, the earth, just like all animals. Indeed, many a person might well wish that there were no resurrection, so that his deeds might never be brought to light.

3. Since it has now graciously pleased the dear God to bring me, an unworthy <term>little worm<note>German: *Würmlein*; a traditional expression of humility used by spiritual writers to emphasize their smallness and dependence on God's majesty.</note></term>, to the knowledge of myself, I have not buried this knowledge in my own <term>self-interest<note>German: *Eigenheit*; in mystical literature, this refers to the state of "selfhood" or the ego-driven life that is separated from God's will.</note></term>. Instead, I share it for the benefit of whoever may find joy in it, and [describe] these figures

4. In my youth, I also strove greatly for this, because I had read in Holy Scripture that Moses, Joshua, David, and other holy men spoke with God. I spent many a half-day walking out alone into the field and looking at the sky, yet I could not reach my goal <note>original: "Intent"; the author's aim or spiritual objective.</note>.

5. Until the merciful God finally appeared to me internally, face to face, and opened His heaven within me, speaking mouth to mouth with my soul <term>essentially<note>original: "entaliter"; pertaining to the very essence of being or substance.</note></term> and <term>mentally<note>original: "mentaliter"; pertaining to the spiritual mind or the higher intellect.</note></term>. This refreshed me more than a little and made me very fiery in my return of love to my dearest Jesus, so that I bound myself to Him with body, soul, and spirit, never to leave Him again, nor to turn away in suffering and tribulation, firmly trusting that He would never take His Spirit from me, nor allow me to be torn away from Him.

6. For He has also faithfully done this: to Him alone be all honor, power, strength, kingdom, and glory for all eternity, Amen!

7. In his outer and inner birth, man is actually a triple constellation <note>German: "dreyfaches Geſtirn"; a central concept in this philosophy suggesting that man is a "microcosm" reflecting three realms: the divine, the physical-astral, and the inner spiritual world.</note>, as can be seen from this figure <note>Refers to an illustration likely present on the original manuscript or printed page.</note>. He lives in a constant driving and hungry desire for his <term>Center<note>Latin: "Centro"; the spiritual source or point of origin where the soul finds its stability and rest.</note></term>, and he always rolls and turns himself anxiously around it, seeking to reach it and find rest within it. And even if he reaches it, he still cannot rest until—

8. The first is the outer **constellation** <note>German: *Gestirn*; in this context, it refers to the physical stars and planets and their perceived influence over human temperament and fate.</note> and the ruler and guide of the natural man, which drives him toward all kinds of arts, crafts, studies, and business; it makes people good and evil, rich and poor, high and low, as is plain to see. Adam fell victim to this governance in his Fall, and brought all his children under it as well, so that in this time—without **rebirth** and a hard struggle—we may not entirely tear ourselves away from it.

9. This life, with its seven forms of outer nature, winds its way into the heart, into the sun, which stands with its root in the fire, from which it receives its radiance and shine. Into these seven forms the Devil, that old serpent, has woven himself, and has sealed and bolted the soul, as can be read in **Revelation 5** <note>Original: *Apoc. 5.*; a reference to the seven-sealed book that only the Lamb of God is worthy to open.</note>.

10. Although, in the state of rebirth, the **Light-World** is in the center, where Jesus has placed himself, having subjected the Fire-World within our humanity to Himself.

12. # Of the Great Mystery

[The soul’s] desire must turn inward and desire God within us, and not desist until the beloved Sophia <note>Divine Wisdom, personified as a feminine figure who guides the soul</note> with the Holy Spirit meets our soul's desire and leads it forward, step by step.

26. For we could not grasp everything at once, because the **Wheel of Nature** <term>Wheel of Nature: A concept in Boehme's thought describing the perpetual, cyclical motion of the forces that drive both the cosmos and the human spirit.</term> turns continuously, and we see the depth of the Godhead only in a dark mirror, as in a fleeting glimpse <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 13:12: "For now we see through a glass, darkly."</note>.

27. This first glimpse is very delicate, and it is impossible to understand everything immediately until the spirit, through long practice, has reached the depths and brought the **Great Mystery** <note>original: "Mysterium magnum"; the hidden source from which all reality emerges</note> to the understanding; as we have learned from our own experience and have also revealed below in the fifth chapter.

28. For it is not as if one simply looks at something and captures its image in his memory to speak of it later: No, the soul must become one **essence** <note>original: "Wesen"; suggesting that the soul does not just learn information, but is physically and spiritually changed into the thing it knows</note> with the knowledge, and pass through the **fire** <term>Fire: The spiritual process of purification and testing that refines the soul's nature.</term> many times before it becomes **fixed** <note>original: "fix"; an alchemical term for a substance that has become stable and can no longer be consumed or changed by heat.</note>.

29. Since intellectual searching is not the most important thing; rather, it is sensing, tasting, and feeling, which no tongue can exalt or express. Even the lover of truth can never grasp the depth merely by reading, unless he himself enters into the essence and follows—through the internal fire—those who have put their experience on paper for him. He will then surely confess himself that he has tasted and sensed it much more deeply in his soul than can be found expressed in books.

30. Truly, we would have no need for any other book in the world besides the Bible, if we only learned to read our own **inner book of the three principles** <term>Three Principles: A core concept in Jacob Boehme’s thought, referring to the three "worlds" or forces that make up reality: the Dark World (divine fire/wrath), the Light World (divine love), and the Outward World (the physical universe).</term> within ourselves: For it is so full of wonders that the human tongue cannot express it, nor can the inexperienced easily believe it.

31. And even the most enlightened writers only stammer when describing it, because they often can find no words to express these things, and must make use of parables from outer nature so that they might provide the seeker with guidance for deeper reflection.

32. But because man has become so entirely external and animalistic, and strives only after the fleeting treasures of this world—loving his own [earthly] life and despising the eternal, imperishable good—it rightly remains sealed to him.

33. For what use is a golden collar to a pig, or a little pearl to a hen? They trample everything into the mire and scrape it away with their feet because they do not recognize it. <note>This is a paraphrase of the biblical warning in Matthew 7:6 regarding "pearls before swine."</note>

34. But to a mind hungry for God and His knowledge, which walks in humility and searches with a simple desire, it happens of itself without great, difficult, or anxious searching. For God is love, and loves the lowly—those who consider themselves entirely unworthy of His great love and are often timid <note>original: "blöd"; in this historical context, it means shy, timid, or simple-minded rather than the modern derogatory sense</note> to take hold of the knowledge of God.

35. God is an enemy to the haughty and proud **spirits of reason** <note>original: "Vernunftsgeistern"; those who rely solely on human logic and intellectualism to understand the divine</note>, and He turns His back on them because they consider themselves wise and learned, wishing to read in God's book of mysteries with their **spectacles of reason** <note>original: "Vernunftsbrille"; a metaphor for the distorted or limited view provided by purely human intellect without spiritual illumination.</note>.

...turn the desire inward and yearn for God within us, and not desist from this until the precious *Sophia* <note>original: "Sophia"; referring to Divine Wisdom, often personified in mystical tradition as a bride or a guiding feminine principle of the soul.</note> with the Holy Spirit meets our soul’s desire and leads it forward step by step.

Of which the first glimpse is very delicate, and it is impossible to understand everything at once until the spirit has come into the depth through long practice and has brought the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium magnum"; the chaotic "ground" or source of all things from which the world was created.</note> to understanding; as we have learned from our own experience and have also revealed somewhat below in the fifth chapter.

28. For it is not as if someone looks at something and captures its image in their memory, from which they can later speak: No, instead the soul must become one essence with the knowledge and pass through the fire many times with it before it becomes fixed <term>fixed: An alchemical term used here to describe spiritual knowledge that has become permanent and stable within the soul, much like a "fixed" metal that resists the heat of a furnace.</term>.

29. For searching is not the most important thing; rather, it is sensing, tasting, and feeling, which no tongue can exalt or express. The lover of these things can never grasp the depth from reading alone unless he himself enters into the essence and follows those inwardly through the fire who bring their experience to paper for him; he will then surely confess for himself that he has tasted and felt it much deeper in his soul than can be found expressed in books.

<summary>This page discusses the necessity of internal spiritual experience over mere intellectual study, emphasizing that the "Great Mystery" must be felt and "fixed" in the soul through the fire of divine experience.</summary>
<keywords>Sophia, Holy Spirit, Wheel of Nature, Godhead, Mysterium Magnum, spiritual experience, alchemy of the soul, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

<page-num>9</page-num>
<header>of divine revelation.</header>

30. Truly, we would have no need for any other book in the world besides the Bible if we only learned to read our own **inner book of the three principles** <term>three principles: A central concept in the author's theology referring to the three "worlds" or dimensions that make up reality: the dark fire-world (the source of strength/wrath), the light world (the source of love/grace), and the external, visible world.</term> within ourselves. For it is so full of wonders that no human tongue can utter them, nor can the inexperienced easily believe them.

31. And even the most enlightened writers only stammer when describing it, for they can often find no words to express these things and must rely on **parables** <note>original: "Gleichnissen"; these are metaphors or analogies drawn from the physical world to explain spiritual truths.</note> drawn from outward nature, so that they might provide guidance to the seeker for deeper meditation.

32. But because man has become so entirely external and animalistic, striving only after the fleeting treasures of this world, loving his own life and despising the eternal, imperishable Good, this book rightly remains sealed to him.

33. For what use is a golden necklace to a pig, or a pearl to a hen? They trample everything into the mire and scratch at it with their feet because they do not recognize its value. <note>This is a reference to the biblical warning in Matthew 7:6 regarding "pearls before swine."</note>

34. To a soul hungry for God and His **knowledge** <note>original: "Erkentniß"; implies a deep, experiential understanding rather than mere factual learning.</note>, however—one that walks in humility and searches with a simple desire—this knowledge meets them of its own accord without great, heavy, or anxious searching. For God is love and loves the lowly who consider themselves entirely unworthy of His great love, and who are often too timid to even grasp the knowledge of God.

35. God is an enemy to the haughty and proud **spirits of reason** <term>spirits of reason: Intellectuals who rely solely on human logic and academic learning (Vernunft) rather than spiritual illumination.</term> and turns His back on them, because they consider themselves wise and learned and wish to read in God’s **book of secrets** <note>original: "Geheimnißbuche".</note> using their own **spectacles of reason** <term>spectacles of reason: A metaphorical warning against trying to perceive divine mysteries through the distorted lens of human ego and logic.</term>.

36. He who asks **God** for His **Holy Spirit**, and will not cease until he is heard, shall find the best and surest way, and receive a guide who will lead him into all depths and unlock all locks and doors, as all enlightened men testify and teach by their own example; apart from this, there is no true finding.

37. Therefore, the hungry seeker must not direct his study toward reading and researching the Scriptures alone; rather, he should consider the imitation of their paths. Alongside diligent prayer, he must set out upon the path itself, hating the earthly life <note>In this mystical context, "hating the earthly life" does not mean literal self-loathing, but rather a detachment from worldly desires and the ego-driven "self-will" that obscures spiritual vision.</note> and seeking the inner life within himself, as I also have done; then he will surely find that their teachings and instructions are from **God**.

38. It is said here, as **Christ** speaks: *He who asks, receives; he who seeks, finds; he who knocks, to him it shall be opened.* For the treasure lies buried deep within the soul and is kept guarded and swallowed up by **God's wrath** <term>God's wrath: Often referred to by the author as the "First Principle" or the "dark fire-world." It is the stern, consuming foundation of existence that remains a source of suffering until it is "transfigured" or overcome by the Light of Divine Love.</term>, which above all things must be overcome through the love of **Jesus**; before then, there is no finding, for that wrath holds fast with great power that which it has swallowed into itself.

39. Therefore, **Christ** calls and teaches us to wrestle and struggle to pass through this **narrow gate**. To this end, an exceedingly great earnestness is necessary, as seen in **Jacob** <note>A reference to Genesis 32, where Jacob wrestles with a divine being until daybreak to receive a blessing.</note>, who held **God** fast by His word—*You, Lord, have said to me: Return again to your land and to your kindred, and I will do you good*, etc.—and did not let it slip away into doubt.

40. # The Second Chapter.

## Introduction.

1. In this accompanying figure <note>The author is referring to an illustration or diagram that would have appeared alongside this text in the original manuscript.</note>—which is the first by our central and enlightened author—the dear reader is shown what the Fall of Adam is; how he broke himself away from the Whole, namely from God and the Jah <note>original: *Jah*. A shortened form of the Divine Name (Yahweh), used here to signify the eternal, unmanifested source of all being.</note>.

2. This wheel turns in good and evil, and reaches only as far as the outer sun <note>In the author's mystical cosmology, the "outer sun" represents the light of the physical, temporal world, which is a mere shadow of the inner, divine Light-World.</note>.

## The Second Chapter

### Preliminary Report

1. In this adjacent figure, which is the first from our central and enlightened author, the dear reader is shown what the Fall of Adam is; how he broke himself off from the Whole, namely from God and the Jah <note>A shortened form of the Hebrew name for God, Yahweh</note>.

2. Since then, he has become a wheel unto himself <note>original: "eigen Rad," a term used in Jacob Boehme’s mysticism to describe a soul that revolves around its own desires rather than God’s light</note>, which now turns within itself through a completely foreign and false essence called lies. These lies were inserted into the souls of our first parents by Satan, that ancient serpent; because of this, our dearest Savior, who is Truth and Life to us, calls Satan a father of lies (John 8:44). This wheel turns within Good and Evil and reaches only as far as the outer sun <note>the physical, material world, which is a mere reflection of the spiritual "inner" sun</note>.

### Concerning the Natural Human.

3. Since the soul is internally dead and is Hell itself, which works eternal destruction and runs headlong into it:

4. Unless a person turns back <note>The German "umkehret" implies a literal turning around, or conversion.</note> in this lifetime and turns their **mind** <term>Gemüth: the seat of the will, emotions, and thoughts; the inner disposition</term> back toward **God**.

5. This is called "doing repentance," the effect of which is in the **mind**—to break out with power from the hellish abyss through prayers, to lay hold of the kingdom of heaven with force, and to seize it for oneself through faith.

6. By this, **rebirth** is understood, where the spark of faith is struck in the **ground of the soul** <term>Seelengrund: a mystical term referring to the deepest, most essential part of a person where they meet God</term>, and the **mind** enters into the **anguish-birth** <term>Angstgeburt: a term from Böhmean mysticism describing the painful, fiery transition from a state of wrath to a state of light</term> within the fire; in this holy burning, confidence and trust in **God** arise, and **Jesus** takes a form in the heart.

7. Through this, faith—in the power of **Jesus**—breaks through all the power of Satan, and gradually tears and breaks all the chains and bonds of wrath and darkness with which the soul had been shackled in all the forms of nature, and throws off the yoke of Satan.

8. This happens through dying, for which purpose **Jesus** grafts us into his death, into a death like His; so that we, with our souls, when they stretch out before **God** with all their might in prayer, become a **tree of the cross** ourselves.

9. And whatever reaches the mystery of this death in their soul rises with a new life in **Christ**, in which righteousness, peace, and joy in the **Holy Spirit** arise, and love comes out of wrath, as is illustrated in the following figure.

The Second Chapter.

## Concerning the Natural Human.

->I.<-

1. If the wisdom-loving reader wishes to search out God in His wonders, and to behold Him in His hidden Trinity <note>original: "Dreyheit"</note> within himself, he must before all things turn inward, and learn to thoroughly recognize himself in his indwelling threefold birth and life; because he is within himself God’s eternal image and likeness, according to the dark-world, fire-world, and light-world <note>These "three worlds" describe the Böhmean spiritual landscape: the dark abyss of potential, the fiery spark of the soul's will, and the light of divine love.</note>.

2. And just as these three are only one world, yet are distinct in their essences and workings; so also is the threefold life in the human being, and none can grasp the other: each stands in its own will and fire or spirit.

3. Each fire also has its own center <term>Centrum: a point of origin or focus in the soul's spiritual anatomy</term>, and desires its own food which is pleasing to it; it does not take in that of another, as can be seen in the different lifestyles and impulses of human beings.

<margin>outer life</margin>
4. The outer life, born forth from the eternal or inner worlds as a likeness <term>Gleichniß: a physical reflection or symbolic representation of a higher spiritual reality</term>, has its center in the outer heart, resides in flesh and blood, and is common to all animals, seeking only to nourish and multiply itself.

<margin>
7 fixed planets
</margin>
5. Its food is the constellation of stars <term>Gestirn: the celestial influences and the physical cosmos</term>, with the elements and the air, which fans the fire in the heart; it has in its wheel the seven forms of outer nature, namely the planets, which give it wit and reason, and govern and drive it; yet it has a temporal beginning and end and must break apart, down to the tincture or essence <term>Tinctur: a term from alchemy used here to describe the refined spiritual life-force or "quintessence" that survives physical death</term>, which persists in the fire; however, that of animals does not.

# Chapter Two.

11. For all self-will is a pure devil; and when the outer life breaks off, the soul remains in the will it held, standing in the dark abyss among the devils.

<margin>

L.L.

12. The third life is the holy Light-life; yet it is hidden in this natural human, inactive and imperceptible: Its fire is God’s fire of love, within which his will burns in those who are reborn <note>Those who have undergone a spiritual transformation or "new birth" in the Böhmean sense.</note>.

13. This also originates in the heart from the fire; but it is one degree deeper than the fire, and in man it is the middle-most, as will be seen in the following chapter.

14. Its food is heavenly essence, Christ’s flesh and blood, and its power-elements in the new body—namely humble love, gentleness, righteousness, truth, etc. Its wheel of birth <term>Wheel of birth: the cyclical process of spiritual manifestation</term> also consists of the seven forms spiritually, but in a state of sinking and humility.

15. Its weaving, driving, and moving is the Holy Spirit, which brings forth heavenly joy from itself; for it gives the soul-fire its gentle water of eternal life for its cooling, and turns anguish into joy.

16. To the outer sensory life, it gives its rays of tincture <term>Tincture: a transformative spiritual essence or "medicine" that can heal and transmute lower states into higher ones</term>; it fends off the poisonous, evil influences of the stars and the devil, from both above and below.

25. This is a very beautiful prototype of the two spiritual fires within us, namely the Fire of Love and the Fire of Wrath: the former is the supernatural fire of God, which comes forth from the inner heaven; the latter is the natural fire of creaturely selfhood in body and soul, awakened by evil desire.

26. And although both are actually only one fire, and differ only in their quality <term>quality: the original German "Qual" implies both a source-property and the "anguish" or "tension" of existence</term>—as can be seen in the relationship between a physical fire and its light—and both were breathed into Adam by God; yet they have moved away from each other through Adam’s earthly desire and false craving, and have been severed from the divine harmony.

27. And now they struggle with one another for dominance within the human being, even within the seed; and whichever gains the upper hand holds the rule in the forming and shaping of the creature in the mother’s womb, as is evident in the cases of Cain and Abel, and also in Esau and Jacob.

28. From this, two kinds of people grow forth, good and evil, and not from a divine providence <note>original: *Versehung*; here used in the sense of predestination.</note> as human reason suggests. For Holy Scripture clearly shows us that God created only One human; though He had plenty of spirit, He sought a divine seed, as seen in Malachi 2:15 and Genesis 1.

## Chapter Two.

35. ...[adorning ourselves] with precious stones, pearls, gold, and silver, and mostly with all kinds of delicious foods, and in order to obtain these things, we often lose our poor soul.

36. And when we have reached the highest point and attained such an earthly paradise, death comes, gives the body to the earth and to the worms for food, and the soul to the hellish dark fire; which for many comes unexpectedly and too soon, nor does it pass without great anguish, as I have observed among the dying.

37. The life-spirit of this earthly man is the air, along with the seven forms, both earthly and <term>sidereal <note>original: "syderisch," referring to the "astral" or "starry" influences that Jacob Böhme believed shaped human temperament and physical life</note></term>; his vision is the light of the sun; his center is the eternal darkness; and where he does not turn toward <term>rebirth <note>original: "Wiedergeburt," the spiritual process of returning the soul to its divine origin</note></term>, the darkness captures him.

38. Because the great <term>starry world-spirit <note>original: "gestirnte Weltgeist," the "Spiritus Mundi" or macrocosmic spirit that governs the physical universe and the elements</note></term> must provide food and drink for man through the elements and raise him to maturity, it desires to hold the supreme command within him with all its power.

39. Although God appointed the soul as the ruler in the body and gave her his heavenly Virgin of Wisdom in the light of life as a helper—so that the soul, using divine wisdom through the external spirit as its instrument, might work and reveal God's wonders in this world; for the wonders of God lie within the <term>fire-soul <note>original: "Feuerseele," the core, energetic essence of the human spirit which Böhme describes as a hunger or fire that must be quenched by Divine Light</note></term>.

40. However, the fire-soul separated itself from its dear helper <note>The "Virgin of Wisdom," or Sophia</note>, wanting to be its own ruler and to work and create whatever it wished in this world; for it believed that the power and strength of the wonders resided within itself.

41. And this is now essentially Adam's fall: He no longer wished to multiply with the **heavenly Virgin** <note>In Böhme’s theology, the "Heavenly Virgin" represents Divine Wisdom (Sophia), with whom man was originally intended to be spiritually united.</note> or to walk in her obedience, but instead, like all animals, desired to have a female mate and to enjoy earthly fruit and pleasure in the body.

42. So God let him fall into sleep, separated the **feminine matrix** <note>original: "Fräuliche Matrix"; referring to the creative, formative womb-principle within the original androgynous human</note> with its tincture of light or water into a woman, and formed for him—through the <term>spirit of the world <note>original: "Spiritum mundi," the macrocosmic spirit that governs the physical elements and the stars.</note></term>—his coarse fleshly body with animal limbs, just as we still are today and as is depicted in this figure.

43. Thus the external **world-spirit** obtained the supreme command within Adam and Eve over the **fire-soul**, and finally, through the serpent’s lying speech and their own earthly desire, brought them to eat from the forbidden tree.

44. In this way, we have become children of this world and lie imprisoned under the yoke of the world-spirit, who now leads us captive as his children with his powerful hunger. Like Pharaoh, he mercilessly drives the children of Israel to his **forced labor** <note>original: "Frohndienst," referring to the heavy, uncompensated labor a serf owed to a feudal lord.</note>, so that we chase after honor, money, splendor, lust, and earthly abundance as if we were to live in this world forever.

45. The soul’s insatiable **greed-hunger** from the dark abyss—upon which this world is founded—contributes to this, driving the soul to beastly immorality, murder, stealing, and all wickedness. These fruits are sown as seeds within the offspring, as can be seen in the firstborn, Cain.

46. Thus the **enemy of mankind** has sown his weeds among the good seed, which now continues to grow until the time of harvest, when God [will gather] the good seed...

47. Fire is very good, and cannot be dispensed with: For it gives warmth to man, and also light, so that he can see in the darkness, and it is useful and most necessary to him for cooking and all kinds of operations.

48. So also is **God’s fire**, when it burns in love, useful and good, and cannot be dispensed with: For it gives man light to see in the darkness; it helps bring God’s wonders to light; it gives the light power and strength, and brings the wonders out of the darkness into being, which would be impossible for the light alone. It awakens joy, exultation, and shouting for joy in heaven; as also painful torment in the darkness.

49. But when the fire wishes to devour all around it, to swallow and annihilate everything combustible, then it is evil; and where it is not extinguished, it burns as long as it finds combustible material; finally it goes out, and becomes a dark coal, ash, and dust. Therefore God also created water, which can resist the fire.

50. So also **God’s wrath-fire** <note>original: "Zornfeuer." In theosophical thought, this refers to the "dark fire" or the harsh, consuming quality of God's nature when separated from His light and love.</note>, when it steps out of its order, breaks away from love, wishes to be a fire of its own, and to swallow all good, it is evil; and if it is not extinguished, it swallows all **oily moisture** <note>original: "ölische Feuchtigkeit." In alchemy and mystical philosophy, this "radical moisture" is the spiritual fuel or "oil" that allows the light of the soul to burn brightly; without it, the soul becomes a dry, dark entity.</note>, so that the light is extinguished, and the fire becomes a dark stump, as the reader sees before his eyes in the second figure.

### The Second Chapter

...wants to live softly without cross and adversity, not put on Christ, nor hunger and thirst for heavenly essence, or incessantly call for strength, ability, help, and assistance.

58. Yes, says the creaturely self-will <note>The "creaturely self-will" refers to the natural human ego that seeks its own comfort and resists divine transformation.</note>: I can do nothing without Christ, nor can I give or take anything for myself out of my own power, nor give anything to God that He might repay me for; if He wants to lead me into the struggle against myself, He must arm me with His spirit of prayer, and also provide the strength and ability so that I can endure. For he who puts his hand to the plow and looks back is unfit for the Kingdom of God <note>A reference to Luke 9:62.</note>; I would rather remain in my simplicity than strive for high things, so I may remain peaceful and quiet; if I am not called at the sixth hour, God can call me at the eleventh hour <note>A reference to the Parable of the Workers in the Vineyard (Matthew 20), used here as an excuse for spiritual procrastination.</note>; he who takes a cross upon himself must carry it afterward; if God wants to lay one on me, He knows well where to find me.

59. Answer: All this is true, dear human, your intention is very good; but you only "devilize" yourself more, covering your conscience with fig leaves <note>A reference to Adam and Eve attempting to hide their shame in Genesis 3:7.</note>, and thinking God does not see your selfhood <term>Eigenheit: the state of being self-centered or acting out of one's own ego rather than God's will</term> in such darkness. If you did not know better, and if God’s will were not revealed to you, you might be able to shield yourself against God's sharp fire-of-wrath <note>original: "Zornfeuer." In Behmenist theology, this is the harsh, consuming aspect of God's power experienced by those who have not turned toward His love.</note>.

60. But you know well that God the Father does not run toward the swineherd <note>A reference to the Prodigal Son in Luke 15, who reached his lowest point while tending pigs.</note>, but toward his turned-around will, which conceives a desire in his heart and wants to go to God with earnestness in humility; even while he is still far off, He runs to meet him, embraces, kisses, and clothes him.

61. Your own will stands well enough, if only it would seize a desire for God, so that God would immediately rush to its aid, and kiss and arm it with His power; but it loves itself far too much, and fears it might cost it its quiet, gentle life in flesh and blood.

## The Second Chapter.

[If it remains] stuck in flesh and blood, and does not press through the fire of anguish <term>Angstfeuer (fire of anguish): In the mystical tradition of Jacob Böhme, this represents the painful but necessary spiritual "heat" where the soul's selfish desires are purified by God's presence.</term> of God's wrath, and let its selfhood be burned away, then it must surely do so after laying aside its earthly tabernacle <note>A biblical metaphor for the human body (2 Corinthians 5:1), suggesting that if one does not undergo spiritual refinement while alive, the process after death will be far more severe.</note>, where it will not go so gently. 67. God indeed breathed into man the free will, with the seven forms <note>original: "sieben Gestalten" — referring to the seven foundational properties of nature which, in this philosophy, constitute the soul's inner workings.</note>, for the out-birthing of the eternal Trinity and divine wisdom; and He ordained for him reason or understanding, with the five counsels or senses, as an external helper, which is called the Mind <note>original: "Gemüth" — the seat of thoughts, will, and disposition.</note>, so that the free will should rule over all creatures and reveal the wonders of God.

68. But only to this end: that he should turn inward, and be subject and obedient to the Maker, and stand resigned in humility in the divine ground of light <term>Lichtsgrund (light-ground): The peaceful, divine essence within the center of the soul where God dwells, contrasted with the dark, restless nature of the physical world.</term>, awaiting what God would work and reveal with and through him; he was to eat from the *Word of the Lord* <note>original: "Verbo Domini"</note> as from the flesh and blood of Christ of the inner man through faith, and not of the earthly fruit, as from the external fleshly body from the maggot-sack <note>original: "Madensack" — a derogatory term used by period mystics to describe the mortal, decaying physical body in contrast to the eternal soul.</note>.

69. Thus this free will of God has broken itself off from the inner ground of light within you, made itself into its own God, and now rules with its forms of outer nature over understanding and the senses; it rides upon the soul, resists and contradicts God in the innermost ground, does what it wants and what is pleasing to the flesh, and is a pure devil, fiery dragon, and serpent, whom God must now wage war against with His strong power of wrath, taming and bending, crushing and casting out.

### Of the Natural Man.

70. Although God, through His Holy Spirit, already wishes to come to the aid of the poor, imprisoned soul and to kindle His divine fire of love within her—so that she might attain a clear radiance and shine, allowing the soul's own self-will to behold its own hideous dragon-shape <note>In this mystical tradition, the "dragon-shape" represents the distorted, monstrous nature of the human ego when it is separated from God's light.</note>;

71. Yet this self-will, through its false desires of the flesh for pleasure, unchastity, honor, wealth, comfortable living, and foolish joy, introduces so much moisture <note>The author uses "moisture" or "dampness" as a metaphor for worldly attachments that dampen the spiritual fire.</note> into the soul-fire that it is smothered, and cannot come to a true burning in God’s love.

72. Even if God calls him to repentance and a turning back <term>Umkehrung: A literal turning around or conversion of the heart.</term> through His reborn children and students, and announces the presence of the Lord to him, this self-will makes excuses. He claims it is not his specific "gift," and that he has received a different gift in which he must work the wonders of God:

73. He argues that all the members of the body are not a single member that performs the same function, but rather many, each of which carries out its own business for the body; or that in a house there are many kinds of wood and beams, where one serves for this burden and another for that.

74. Thus he hardens himself within himself, and shuts the door of the mind <note>original: "Gemüth" — the seat of the soul’s disposition and thoughts.</note> to the divine voice, so that he is not disturbed in his fleshly peace and self-rule.

75. Who, then, will punish and convict this "beautiful god of self"? For he is hidden within a tame animal, under a human mask <note>original: "Larve" — a mask or deceptive outer shell; the idea that a person appears civil on the outside while remaining spiritually wild.</note>; he is outwardly pious, honorable, and devoted to religious service; he goes into...

# Chapter Two.

75. [continues into] the churches and to the Lord’s Supper, contributes to the care of teachers and the poor, boasts of Christ’s baptism and of being destined for salvation, or hopes to be saved by grace.

76. He knows how to speak from the Scriptures as well as his teachers; indeed, he may go so far as to leave the external church, condemn its decay, and seek the acquaintance of righteous and earnest disciples of Christ, investigating their hidden life and language. He might even practice external circumcision, abstain from his wife, or remain unmarried, speaking also of the chaste marriage bed of *Sophia* <note>original: "Sophia" — Divine Wisdom, often personified as a spiritual bride in this mystical tradition.</note> and the marriage with the faithful soul.

# On the Natural Man.

[...an eye-sore] <note>This continues the thought from the end of page 30, where the "natural man" views Christ-like poverty as an offense.</note> greatly, and it makes the man so stone-blind that he can no longer recognize God's love, nor distinguish it from his own self-love. 81. But your own will is not God’s prophet **Elijah**, who was subject to the Spirit of God; rather, it is only a self-will of the devil, which has seated itself in the light of your life as if in God’s temple, and rules over everything that is God’s, believing that it is God itself.

82. Blind <term>Reason: "Vernunft" in this tradition refers to human logic and intellect that operates without divine inspiration, often used as an excuse to avoid spiritual sacrifice.</term> wants to speak in favor of this self-will and says: "If all people were to walk in **Christ’s footsteps** and follow Him in total conformity, the world could not endure, because all livelihoods would come to a standstill."

83. 84. 85. Instead, it should say: "Lord, it is yours..."

# Chapter Two.

87. # The Second Chapter.

95. My answer, however, is that Reason <note>In this context, "Reason" (Vernunft) is personified as a worldly, limited way of thinking that cannot grasp spiritual truths.</note> is blind and does not know the poverty of Christ, nor how to distinguish it from worldly poverty: For Christ’s poor are faithful Christians who are poor solely out of love for their head, Christ, and value such poverty more highly than all the wealth of the world.

96. 97. A believer does not make himself poor; rather, the world persecutes him, takes everything from him, and leaves him nothing at all, banishing him from the land and thus bringing him into the true, genuine poverty of **Jesus**, so that he can neither buy nor sell in the world:

98. And thus, as if forced—though joyful and willing—he must become Christ’s student and seek **God’s** kingdom in the firm trust that **God** will provide for clothing and necessities.

99. In which faith he must also faithfully endure without looking back until his end, or faith will be taken from him, and he must beg and be a slave to the world and his belly <note>original: "Bauchs"; referring to someone driven only by physical needs and base appetites.</note>.

100. Now, if **God** did not care for such children and members of His household, He would be worse than a heathen <note>A term used here for a non-believer or someone outside the faith community.</note>, even worse than a wild animal that provides for its young.

105. A faithful Christian renounces all trust in people, even in his own hands, for **God** has cursed such trust: an unbelieving worldly-poor person cannot reach this state, for he has no other **God** or helper than the rich of this world.

106. ### Concerning the Natural Human Being.

113. Since I had also completely intended to make no acquaintances with any person—because I was fearful of **Reason**, who is today the ruler of the world and knows how to master everything cleverly, and I foresaw well what a fierce struggle would grow for me from human acquaintance.

114. But I had to defer to the **Spirit of God**—who revealed my conduct and life, against my will, to a good friend in prayer—and I had to entrust such things to Him.

115. I have the **Highest** to thank, who wonderfully snatched my life, which I have presented in **Christ** <note>original Latin: "Christo"</note>, out of the jaws of death and hell against all human expectation, and did not let me succumb in this fiery struggle with **God** and men.

116. Only it is painful that among thirty, not more than one stood firm in will and grasped **Jesus** in faith, who alone can praise the omnipotence of the love of **God**.

117. **Reason** speaks: "Since you speak and boast of such a high Apostolic life, why then do you not also go out into the world to preach the Gospel, but instead sit still in corners and do not put **God’s** gifts to interest?" <note>original: "wuchert Gott nicht," a reference to the Parable of the Talents, where servants are expected to "trade" or "put to usury" the capital given to them by their master.</note>

### The Second Chapter.

...what He wishes to do or leave undone with and through our will, as His instrument.

120. Running and racing on one's own, even with good intentions, does not suffice, and only works perversely against **God's** will. A still, surrendered <term>Gelassen: a central Pietist concept of yielding one's will to God, often translated as "resigned" or "equanimous"</term> will is more useful and pleasing to **God** than a self-willed, self-active one.

121. We want only and alone what **God** wants; we remain in that, and let **Reason** judge us as it pleases, for we know that it is blind in divine matters.

122. Now we know that **God** is good, and everything He has made was good; therefore, we can in no way blame **God** for the fact that we have become evil, having withdrawn from Him, the **Maker**, the divine free will He breathed into us, and making it our own.

123. This [self-will] is now a pure devil, which the **Spirit of God**—in the books of **Daniel** and **John**—has compared to horrible ravenous beasts, as well as abominable dragons, poisonous snakes, and other creeping things; certainly, this was not done out of hatred, envy, or bitterness: <note>The author refers to the apocalyptic visions in the Old Testament Book of Daniel and the New Testament Book of Revelation, traditionally attributed to John.</note>

124. Having previously described the fallen nature of the self-will as a "beast" or "dragon," he now examines whether a soul, once in the state of eternal "wrath-fire," can ever change its nature or be redeemed.</meta>

<header>Of the Natural Man.</header>

...which depends neither on **God's** omnipotence, nor on our will; but rather on the will of the damned souls and the devil—whether they can ever, in eternity, conceive a will toward humility, and should desire to be delivered from the **God’s** <term>Zornfeuer: "Wrath-fire," a term used by mystics like Jacob Böhme to describe the harsh, consuming aspect of God’s power experienced by those alienated from His love</term>.

126. For my part, I would not like to risk relying upon that, since I believe that whatever is eternal is no longer subject to any change. I am not writing here of those who remain hanging by a thread, and must pass through the magical fire of wrath with unspeakable pain: <note>This likely refers to a state of purification or purgatory for those not fully lost.</note>

127. But rather of those upon whom no intercession will be able to take hold, and for whom no relief in the fire is to be hoped: these will immediately renounce **God**, curse Him, and flee. Good intentions and one's own will will find no place in **God’s** judgment; instead, each must be satisfied with **God's** verdict; forasmuch as **God** is righteous and true.

128. And even if one wished to draw a conclusion from 1 Peter 3:19 <note>The passage reads: "By which also he went and preached unto the spirits in prison."</note> regarding a certain redemption from Hell, because **Christ** preached to the spirits in the prison; yet I confess that I have been in the spirit both in this prison between time and eternity, and in the Hell of the devils, but I found a great difference between the two.

129. For in the prison between time and eternity there is no agonizing torment, and it is neither dark nor light; but rather a <term>Demmerung: Twilight or dusk; a middle state of the soul</term>. In Hell, however, there is nothing but despair and terrible agonizing torment, which immediately forces the soul into despair and the renunciation of **God**.

<sig>C 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: 130.</meta>

<summary>The author argues that while some souls may experience a "twilight" state after death, those in the eternal "Wrath-fire" of Hell are beyond redemption because their suffering forces them to eternally reject God. He distinguishes this absolute Hell from the biblical "prison" where Christ preached, based on his own spiritual visions.</summary>

# The Second Chapter.

130. For my stay therein lasted only six hours; and if **God** had not held my tongue, I would have immediately renounced Him forever. <note>The author suggests the spiritual terror of this "prison" was so great that, without divine intervention, he would have despaired and turned away from God entirely.</note>

131. However, I should also not conceal how **God** has let me observe—during the passing of great and heavy sinners whom I was called to attend—that **Christ** pursues the soul right up until the separation of body and soul.

132. And when the soul reaches the destination of the <term>eighth fire-form: "Achthe Feuersgestalt," a term likely drawn from the mystical chemistry of Jacob Böhme, representing a stage of spiritual trial or the boundary of the soul's inner fire</term>, wherein **Moses** stands with his fiery law and the **Devil** reads the register of sins to the soul, **Christ** stands as a Mediator and <term>Mercy Seat: "Gnadenstuhl," the golden cover of the Ark of the Covenant, symbolizing the place where judgment is met with atonement</term> to be the soul's advocate, so that it might still enjoy one look of grace and remain hanging by the thread of hope.

133. But I would advise no one to confidently go on sinning because of this: for where the wrath of **God** is too greatly heaped up, it burns with unspeakable sharpness, as can be seen by the analogy of a natural fire: the more wood and oil one pours on, the hotter it burns. Therefore, let everyone be warned.

134. Now, when one considers how all the religions in the world have sharpened their intellects to pave a gentle path to Paradise for this <term>beast-devil: "Thierteufel," the author's term for the fallen, animalistic nature of the human ego</term>, attempting to lead it into the glory of **God** without the <term>dying-away of the self: "Absterbung der Selbheit," the mystical process of surrendering one's own will and ego to God</term> and the following of **Christ**; then one sees the conflict of the <term>three principles: "dreyen Principien," a cornerstone of Böhme’s cosmology referring to the Dark World (wrath), the Light World (love), and the Outward World (nature)</term> for sovereignty over the soul, and how they have always wrestled with each other and stood side by side.

135. From this, the blindness and great self-centeredness of the natural man have arisen; since—

<summary>The author describes Christ's role as a mediator who pursues even the greatest sinners until the moment of death, while warning that continued sin fuels the "fire" of divine wrath. He critiques institutional religions for trying to find a way to salvation that avoids the difficult process of surrendering the ego.</summary>

# On the Natural Human.

...he believes that his own doctrine and opinion must be **God’s** yardstick, according to which He should judge, and not **God’s** Word and commandment.

136. For here we are not thinking of the heathens, who have heard nothing of **Christ**: a mindset which persists among Christians to this very hour; wherein everyone lives and dies according to their own opinion, and thinks they shall sit and rule at the right hand of **God** in heaven alongside **Christ**, without [experiencing] the rebirth and the dying away of <term>selfhood: "Eigenheit," a term used by mystics like Jacob Böhme to describe the ego or the self-centered will that separates humans from God</term>, just as they are led and instructed by their blind teachers.

<margin><insert>J. B.</insert></margin> <note>These initials refer to Jacob Böhme (1575–1624), the German shoemaker and mystic whose complex cosmological visions deeply influenced later Pietist and Romantic thought.</note>
137. Since the great **God** took pity on our human misery, He has by His grace—through the simple, unlearned layman *Jacob Böhme*—exposed the deep ground of the <term>three principles: "dreyen Principien," Böhme’s model of reality consisting of the dark fire-world (God's wrath/power), the light world (God's love), and the outward world (nature)</term>, as the tree of the knowledge of good and evil, and the resulting fall of **Lucifer** and **Adam**.

138. This knowledge has ignited many a soul to dig further for this <note>original: "Perlein"</note> little pearl within themselves; then one here, another there, has found a small vein [of truth] which has driven them toward the love of **God** and prayer. <note>The "pearl" refers to the "Pearl of Great Price" from the Gospels, symbolizing the inner spark of divine life or the Kingdom of God hidden in the heart.</note>

139. Although his writings only came into my hands some years after my <term>awakening: "Aufweckung," a term for the profound moment of spiritual conversion or enlightenment</term>, they have provided very great benefit in my earnest struggle.

140. And it is truly a cause for wonder that people have deviated so quickly from the great zeal for the following of **Christ** begun by the early Church, and have fallen back from the renunciation of all earthly goods into a self-centered comfort.
