# Chapter Four.

...will come into camp <note>original: "lager kommen," likely meaning a state of rest, safety, or spiritual victory</note> before he has completed his schooling <note>original: "Schulrecht gethan," referring to the rigorous spiritual discipline, testing, and "tuition" required of a student of God's ways.</note> and has overcome the Prince of Fury or the Red Dragon <note>A biblical reference to Satan (Revelation 12:3), representing the wrathful, fire-nature of the fallen soul that must be tamed.</note> and has bound him within himself.

111. For the devil acts just like soldiers who storm a fortress blindly; even if they are beaten back many times and must lose their lives in the attempt, they still hope to finally weary the defender.

112. Therefore, an earnest suitor <note>original: "Werber," meaning one who woos or seeks the hand of Divine Wisdom (Sophia).</note> must not be idle or sleep; rather, he must always be on his guard, standing in constant fear, and look upon everything that the devil presents to him in the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spiritu Mundi"; the mundane consciousness or the atmospheric spirit that governs worldly desires and human reasoning.</note>—even if it appears to be godly and does not run exactly against the clear letter of Scripture—as nothing but temptations, and quickly reject them without speculation <note>original: "Speculirung," the act of over-analyzing, intellectualizing, or ruminating on spiritual experiences rather than resting in simple faith.</note>.

113. And even if a loud inner objection should arise within him from the **Spirit of the World**, he must lend no ear to it at all, but diligently pray against it, and let it be his one, firm, and unchangeable purpose nevermore to leave his Jesus and his most beloved Bride;

114. But rather to hate everything, even his own life, and to entirely flee and avoid whatever could entice him away from this chaste love into a creaturely love <note>Love directed toward created things or people rather than toward the Creator.</note>.

115. Had I not practiced this with great earnestness for over 30 years, I would never have come to this certainty in which I stand through God’s grace, and the devil would have mocked Christ and all his dear children, and led my soul back into his dark prison, from which Christ graciously led me with his blood and death—

<center>### Concerning the Inner Man, etc. ###</center>

...led me away, locked me out, and have darkened my beautiful pearl <note>original: "Perlein," a common mystical metaphor for the spark of God or Divine Wisdom within the soul.</note> once again.

116. But it is better to lose earthly life than to lose **Christ**; He can give us eternal life in exchange for it.

117. The life of **Jesus Christ** is quite contrary to the natural life of this world-rule <term>Weltregel: the governing principles, systems, or "spirit" of the material world</term>; therefore, He is also a stranger to all natural men through His children.

118. Just as **Christ** Himself points out in **Matthew 25**, where the rich <note>The text specifies "the rich" (die Reichen), though the biblical passage usually addresses those at the Last Judgment; the author likely identifies the worldly-wise and wealthy as those who fail to recognize Christ in the poor.</note> speak and answer: Lord, when did we see you hungry or thirsty, sick, naked, or in prison?

119. For what the natural man loves, a Christian hates; what the worldly people possess, the Christians renounce. For their conduct is in the will or the heaven of the mind <term>Gemüthshimmel: a mystical term referring to the inner spiritual realm where the soul interacts with God</term>, with God and all the holy angels.

120. They seek only God’s kingdom and what is above, and do not trouble themselves with the business of sustenance <note>original: "Nahrungs-händeln," meaning commercial trades or the anxious pursuit of worldly livelihood</note>; nor do they seek treasures for which thieves dig; much less do they worry about what they shall eat, drink, or put on their bodies, even though the earthly body still clings to them as it does to all men.

121. For this reason, they are everywhere regarded as foolish and insane, even as murderers of their own lives, who despise God's gifts created for daily benefit and use, and are seen as only a useless, ruinous, and harmful plague in good, well-ordered *republics* <note>original: "Republiquen," referring to civil societies or states</note>;

122. Who provide benefit to no one, but only consume the good stores <note>original: "Vorrath," the resources or collective provisions of a community</note> and are a hindrance and harm to other good people; [consuming] the rich...

111. For the Devil acts just like soldiers who blindly storm a fortress; even if they are beaten back many times and must lose their lives in the process, they still hope to eventually exhaust the defender.

112. 113. 114. But rather to hate everything, even his own life, and to entirely flee and avoid whatever might lead him away from this chaste love into a natural <note>meaning carnal or worldly</note> love.

115. 120. They seek only God’s kingdom and what is above; they do not concern themselves with the trade of sustenance <note>original: *Nahrungshändeln*; the business and anxieties of securing daily food and living</note>, nor do they seek treasures for which thieves dig; even less do they worry about what they shall eat, drink, or put on their bodies, even though the earthly body still clings to them as it does to all people.

121. # The Fourth Chapter.

110. [...reach his] camp <note>a place of spiritual rest or the soul's final destination</note> before he has completed his apprenticeship <note>original: *Schulrecht gethan*; a metaphor from the guild system meaning to have finished one's required training or trials</note> and has overcome and bound within himself the Prince of Wrath or the Red Dragon <note>referring to the devil or the wrathful, fallen nature as described in the Book of Revelation</note>.

111. For the devil acts just like soldiers who blindly storm a fortress; even though they are repelled many times and must lose their lives in the process, they still hope to eventually wear down the protector.

112. Therefore, an earnest seeker <term>seeker: original *Werber*, meaning one who woos or petitions; here, a soul seeking union with Divine Wisdom</term> must not be lazy or sleep; instead, he must always be on his guard and stand in constant fear. Everything the devil presents to him in the Spirit of the World <term>Spirit of the World: the *Spiritu Mundi*, the external influence of the material cosmos and social systems</term>, even if it appears to be divine and does not directly contradict the clear letter of Scripture, he must view as nothing but temptations and quickly reject them without speculation <note>original: *Speculirung*; here meaning intellectual curiosity or "reasoning" that distracts from simple, heart-centered faith</note>.

113. And even if a loud protest should arise within him from the Spirit of the World, he should lend it no ear, but instead diligently pray against it. Let it be his sole, firm, and unchangeable purpose never to leave his **Jesus** and most beloved bride <note>the "bride" is a traditional mystical symbol for the soul's union with God or Divine Wisdom</note>;

114. But rather to hate everything, even his own life, and to completely flee and avoid whatever might entice him from this chaste love into a natural love <note>affection directed toward worldly "creatures" or material things rather than the Creator</note>.

115. Had I not practiced this with great earnestness for over 30 years, I would never have arrived at this certainty in which I stand through **God’s** grace. The devil would have mocked **Christ** and all His dear children, and would have led my soul back into his dark prison, from which **Christ** through His blood and death graciously led: <meta>catchword: led:</meta>

### Chapter Four.

[...swallow] sweat and blood, and through idleness and sinful sloth squander the basic necessities <term>necessities: original *Nothdurft*, referring to the bare essentials required to sustain life, such as food and clothing</term> of other needy people;

123. With such people, according to Paul’s admonition <note>referring to the Apostle Paul; original: *Pauli Vermahnung*</note>, one should have nothing to do: *If anyone does not obey our word in this epistle, note that person and do not keep company with him* <note>original: 2 Thess. 3, 14</note>. This is because they infect others with their poisonous teaching and create so many poor people that even the rich themselves would not be able to enjoy their own necessities if they were required to support all the poor.

124. Such "hard food" <note>a metaphor for the difficult trials or harsh treatment a believer must endure</note> is indeed quite indigestible to a poor Christian stomach; yet one must make do with it and think: "The servant is not better than his Master." <note>A reference to Matthew 10:24, suggesting that if Christ suffered, His followers should expect the same.</note>

125. If they did not recognize the Lord in the world, how then should they recognize His despised, poor members <note>the "members" of the Body of Christ, i.e., other believers</note>, who are full of infirmities and clothed in an earthly, dark body like all other men? It requires different eyes to recognize a poor Christian.

126. When **God** drives a person out of the world and calls them away from their field and plow into **Christ’s** life and discipleship, then that person must keep themselves entirely blind, deaf, and dumb to outward things;

127. And they must certainly not look around with their eye of reason <term>eye of reason: original *Vernunftsaug'*; the human intellect or logical mind, which the author views as limited compared to spiritual intuition</term> at the common course of the world, which exists under a regime entirely its own and foreign to the life of **Jesus**; otherwise, one can very easily and quickly be "impregnated" <note>here meaning to be filled with or influenced by worldly ideas</note> and led astray.

128. Instead, he must only turn his inward eye toward his predecessor and leader **Jesus** in the heart, and pay close attention to how He wishes to will, work, and move within the soul.

### Concerning the Battle of Michael and the Dragon.

1.
This spiritual struggle between love and wrath, light and darkness, Yes and No, already arose in heaven before the creation of the visible world within **Lucifer’s** own creaturely will:

2. Who broke away from his origin as eternal love, introduced himself into a self-willing, working, and shaping, and sought to fight against **God** and his Son of love, and also to force his other fellow brothers, the dear angels, into apostasy from love along with him; but they triumphed.

3. And **Lucifer** with his angels was cast out of heaven onto the earth, as John reveals in <note>original: "Apoc. 12. Capitel"; the Book of Revelation, chapter 12, which describes the war in heaven</note>.

4. Then this fierce prince of wrath sought his fortune afterward upon **Adam**, who had been set in his place as the <term>prince of the throne: a title suggesting that humanity was intended to inherit the high spiritual rank vacated by the fallen angels</term> and human <term>hierarch: a leader of a sacred order or a bridge between the divine and the worldly</term> in these

It frames the fall of the angels as a rejection of divine love in favor of "self-willing" and positions humanity as the intended replacement for the fallen heavenly hosts.</summary>

10. Because there are some obscure expressions in Holy Scripture which certainly require a good explanation, unenlightened reason grasps them with great misunderstanding to its own advantage,

13. Now, it is certainly true that no thing can become manifest in itself without <term>opposition: a state of contrariety or resistance</term> <note>original: "Widerwärtigkeit"</note>; also, if there were no opposition in life, there would be neither sensitivity nor will, neither understanding nor <term>knowledge: in the sense of divine science or awareness</term> <note>original: "Wissenschaft"</note> within it.

14. For a single thing knows nothing more than one [thing]; and even if it is inherently good, it knows neither evil nor good, because it has nothing within itself that makes it sensitive <note>The author argues that without a "second" thing to provide contrast, the first thing cannot perceive its own nature.</note>.

15. Thus we can likewise philosophize about the will of **God** and say: If the hidden **God**, who is but a single being and will, had not led Himself out of Himself through His will—out of the eternal knowledge in equality into the <term>differentiation: the state of being divided or distinct</term> <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit"</note> of the will, and had not introduced such differentiation into a natural and created life within a <term>comprehensibility: the state of being contained or graspable</term> <note>original: "Infaßlichkeit"</note>, and if that same differentiation in life did not stand in conflict;

<summary>This page argues that the apparent "opposition" or evil in the world is not caused by God, but is a necessary condition for consciousness and life to manifest from the unity of the Divine. ...how then would the hidden will of **God**, which in itself is only One, be manifest to Him? <note>This sentence completes the rhetorical question from the previous page: if God were only a hidden, undifferentiated unity, He would have no mirror in which to perceive His own nature.</note>

16. But where in the single will there is a <term>differentiation: a state of being divided or distinct</term> <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit"</note>, and there is also a self-will within that which is separated, and thus within a single will unfathomable and innumerable wills arise, like branches from a tree:

17. Thus we see and understand that in such differentiation, every separated will leads itself into its own form, and that the conflict of wills is about the form—that in the division, one form is not like the other, even though they all stand in one <term>ground: the underlying foundation or essence</term> <note>original: "Grund"</note>.

18. Just as evil or the <term>contrary-will: an opposing or resisting will</term> <note>original: "Widerwille"</note> causes the good will to press back toward its <term>original state: its primordial source or station</term> <note>original: "Urstand"</note> as toward **God** and to become desiring: (For that which is only good in itself, and has no <term>torment: the friction or tension necessary for life and perception</term> <note>original: "Qual"</note>, desires nothing, because it knows nothing better within or before itself for which it could long:)

19. So one can also say of the single good will of **God**, that He can desire nothing in Himself; since He has nothing within or before Him that could give Him anything.

20. For which reason He leads Himself out of Himself into a differentiation, so that in that which has <term>flowed forth: the created or emanated world</term> <note>original: "Ausgeflossenen"</note> an opposition might arise, and the good might become sensitive, active, and willing within the evil; namely, to separate itself from the evil, and to will to enter again into the single will of **God**.

...[flows] <term>divine power: the active energy of the Godhead</term> <note>This completes the sentence from the previous page: "So one can also say of the single good will of God, that it flows..."</note> out of the **Eternal One** into <term>differentiation: the state of being divided or distinct</term> <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit"</note> and into many <term>centers: focal points of life or will</term> <note>original: "Centra"</note>,

22. And, through its movement, causes the "Good" within itself to long for <term>stillness: the peace of the original unity</term> once again, and to become desiring to penetrate back into the **Eternal**: And in such an operation exists sensitivity, knowledge, and the act of willing.

23. **God**, insofar as He is called **God**, has nothing before or after Him that He could desire or will; but where He does will something, that very thing has flowed out from Him and is an <term>opposition: a mirror-image or object of contemplation</term> <note>original: "Gegenwurf"</note> of Himself, in which the eternal will wills within its own "Something."

24. Now, if this "Something" were only a single thing, the will would have no way to accomplish anything within it; and for that reason, the <term>groundless will: the will that exists before any cause or foundation</term> <note>original: "ungründliche Wille"</note> divided itself in the beginning and clothed itself in essence, so that it might be active within "Something"; just as one sees an analogy in the **mind** of man.

25. If the **mind** did not flow out from itself, it would have no **senses**; and if it had no **senses**, it would have no knowledge of itself, nor of any other thing, and could perform no work or accomplishment.

26. The sensory outflow from the **mind** makes the **mind** willing or desiring to lead the **senses** into an <term>I-ness: a sense of self or individual identity</term> <note>original: "Ichheit"</note>; wherein the **mind** works through the **senses**, and reveals and beholds itself in that working.

27. If all **senses** were but one single **sense**, they would also have only one will, and would always do only one thing: How then would the wonders and powers of divine **wisdom** be recognized through the **mind** and expressed in forms?

### The Fifth Chapter.

28. Because, however, an <term>opposition: a state of conflict or contrast</term> <note>original Latin: "Contrarium"</note>, such as light and darkness, is within it, this very opposition is contrary to itself; because of this, one property always causes the other to enter into desire, to struggle against the other, and to want to rule over it.

29. Within this desire, the senses and the <term>disposition: the inner emotional and mental core of a person</term> <note>original: "Gemüth"</note> are led into a natural and created foundation toward a state of "willing"—specifically, as a desire for mastery within its own "Something," as if wanting to rule over all others with the sense of its own disposition.

30. Therefore conflict and anguish, as well as aversion, arise in the disposition, so that the entire disposition is moved to enter once more into a breaking-apart of the senses and of the self-will of the senses, and to lead itself back into **God**, from whom it first originated.

31. From this arises faith and hope: that the anxious disposition hopes for a redemption and yearns again for its origin, which is **God**.

32. This is also how divine revelation is to be understood; for all things have their first beginning from the <term>outflow: the emanation or flowing-forth of divine energy</term> <note>original: "Ausfluß"</note> of the divine will, whether it be good or evil, love or sorrow.

33. And yet, since the will of **God** is no "thing"—neither nature nor creature—it contains no pain, sorrow, nor aversion; instead, understanding and knowledge have flowed out from the outflow of the <term>Word: the creative expression of God</term>.

# The Fifth Chapter.

67. He will be enlightened by **GOD** to understand and recognize that his own will is that fiery dragon of <note>original: "Apoc. 12."</note> Revelation 12, with which the inner man must struggle through the power of **JESUS**,

69. Regarding this struggle, **GOD** already spoke to our first parents in <note>original: "Gen. 3."</note> Genesis 3: I will put enmity between you, as the serpent-devil, and the woman, and between your seed and her seed.

70. This struggle is also presented by the Spirit of **JESUS** in Revelation 12; it has lasted from Adam until this very moment, and will likely continue in the faithful until the very end.

71. Since this struggle is spiritual, and occurs both internally between spirit and flesh within the senses, as well as externally between the seed of the woman and the seed of the serpent, I will share with the reader what the dear **GOD** grants me for their benefit, so far as my own experience reaches.

72. My Christian reader should be informed, however, that I write here of reborn minds who, like the <term>prodigal son: a reference to the biblical parable of the wayward son who returns to his father</term> <note>original: "verlornen Sohn"</note>, are on the way to their dear Father's house.

<meta>catchword: 73.</meta>

<summary>This page identifies the human "self-will" as the red dragon of Revelation and explains that spiritual peace is only possible once this ego is defeated. It frames this internal struggle as a lifelong spiritual battle rooted in the original promises of Genesis.</summary>

# Of the Battle Between Michael and the Dragon.

73. To the one who is earthly-minded, however, I have written nothing; he will not understand it: for an earthly person lives according to the lusts of the flesh and knows of no practice other than external happenings.

74. But a reborn person, who breaks out of the false lust of the flesh as if from the devil’s <note>original: "Saustall"</note> pigsty, and sets his senses toward the <term>Holy of Holies: the innermost sacred chamber of the soul where God is said to dwell</term> <note>original: "Allerheiligste"</note>, where **GOD** dwells and is present to the soul—he is tempted by the devil through false whispering into the <term>mind: the inner disposition or seat of thoughts and emotions</term> <note>original: "Gemüth"</note>. From this, a false *imagination* <note>original: "Imagination"; in this context, it refers to the spiritual power to form mental images or desires</note>, offensive false thoughts, and aversion originate.

75. For the outer mind stands half in darkness and half in light, as is to be seen in the figures of the third chapter;

76. And it must suffer much evil within itself against its will; and although the devil cannot see into the light-part of the soul, he nevertheless lurks in the dark part for the intentions of the will, creating a mixture and confusion in the <term>wheel of the mind: the cyclical, restless nature of human thoughts and desires</term> <note>original: "Rad des Gemüths"</note> and the senses, and hindering everything good wherever he can.

77. Concerning this, Paul and all the saints have lamented at all times, that they often do what they do not want to do. <note>A reference to the Apostle Paul's struggle in Romans 7:15.</note>

78. Therefore, if we wish to rightly understand the ground of this spiritual battle, we must search in the <term>light of nature: the innate capacity to understand spiritual and physical truths through divine illumination and observation</term> <note>original: "Licht der Natur"</note> to see what our mind is, from which love and anger, light and darkness, joy and sorrow can arise in a single moment;

79. Yes, faster than a flash of lightning, both good and evil thoughts diverge within us without number:

## The Fifth Chapter.

80. Thus we will find that it is a **desiring will** <note>original: "begehrender Wille"</note> which stands imprisoned in the darkness, and always longs for the light, and fixes its will upon the light to bring it forth, so that it may be redeemed from the anxious torment and may reach the <term>paradise or temperament: a state of perfect inner balance and harmony where the conflicting spiritual forces are reconciled</term> <note>original: "Paradis oder Temperament"</note> within itself;

81. Wherein it may rest from the **anxious driving** <note>original: "ängstlichen Treiben"</note> and stand still in its anxious desire, so that **GOD’S** will may proceed within it, as has already been detailed in §. 25: 31.

82. Although an unpracticed person needs even more instruction and guidance, who shall be helped by the presentation of a **parable** <note>original: "Gleichniß"</note>.

83. The <term>mind: the seat of inner thoughts, emotions, and spiritual intent</term> <note>original: "Gemüth"</note> hungers and vehemently desires to bring forth the light within itself; and the more vehement the desire is, the greater becomes the dark, anxious torment, so that unpracticed minds often fall into great <term>despondency: a state of spiritual doubt or losing heart</term> <note>original: "Zweifelmüthigkeit"</note> and let their courage sink;

84. They also depart from prayer and the struggle; as I have also done, and at the same time advise my dear reader—not that he should lose heart and abandon the struggle;

85. But rather that he take up another **will**, to break out of this anxious, painful darkness, leaving the first will alone in the darkness.

86. Thus he will well experience, in earnest prayer and perseverance, what this pen cannot express.

87. ### The Fifth Chapter.

### Chapter Five.

...despised my sincere admonitions, separated himself in a reckless fit of wrath, and scattered once again what he himself had previously gathered together;

94. That among thirty, only one single person remained steadfast, who risked life and limb, and received the seal of the Holy Spirit.

97. 98. And we must do this constantly, until love overcomes the wrath, and has <term>transmuted: a term from alchemy meaning to change one substance into another, higher form</term> <note>original: "transmutiret"</note> and changed it into love as well.

99. ...shall desire that it should be given to us by our heavenly Father.

135. **God** cannot possibly lie; therefore, if we ask and do not receive, it is purely our own fault, because either our **soul-tincture** <term>Soul-tincture (Seelentinctur): A term from spiritual alchemy referring to the essential quality or inner "coloring" of the soul; for prayer to be effective, this essence must be aligned with the divine light.</term> is not entirely and fully placed in **God**, or otherwise it hangs onto something earthly, or looks toward a creature <note>In this mystical context, "creature" refers to any created thing or person as opposed to the Creator.</note>.

136. Even a dog does not abandon its young; how then should **God** abandon those who call to Him day and night, and trust in Him with their whole heart?

137. And although at times in temptations it seems as if heaven were steel and iron, and **God** had forgotten to hear; yet the mind must not be terrified nor faint, or doubt, and cease from prayer;

138. But rather persevere with right fiery earnestness, and like **Jacob** wrestle with **God** <note>A reference to Genesis 32, where Jacob wrestles with a divine figure until he receives a blessing.</note> until **God** and men are overcome, and faith together with love has triumphed, as we have a glorious example in the patient **Job**.

139. Here, it represents the internal spiritual barrier that keeps the soul in doubt.</note> will keep his mind and senses imprisoned in constant doubt and unrest, and never let him come to a certainty within himself, nor allow him to break out of the dark prison of the **wrath** <term>Wrath (Zorn): In the theology of Jacob Böhme and his followers, this refers to the "dark fire" or the harsh, judging quality of God that must be transformed by love.</term>.

140. The strong armed man guards his fortress and kingdom mightily: We must have the Hero **Jesus** with us in the battle, and the Hea-

141. No conflict is more violent, nor more distressing or painful, than when the good **God** unites good, dear souls in one love, heart, and will, and one of them allows a bitter root to grow up in his heart:

142. From this arises a division of the single Will of Love into many bitter wills, and a separation of principles; just as it happened in heaven among the dear angels, when finally **Lucifer** with his host was driven out of the light and into the darkness;

143. And so it also arose among my **playmates** <note>original: "Mitgespielen". 145. Yes, of their own free will, they bound themselves to one another three times by hand and mouth, so that to preserve such precious **brotherly love**, each must guard himself against bitterness and immediately curse the slightest thought that was not love at its very first stirring;

# Chapter Five.

147. From this arose such a bitter **dragon-will** <note>In the mystical tradition of Jakob Böhme, the "dragon-will" refers to the selfish, fierce, and prideful nature of the fallen soul that rebels against God's light.</note> and devil, that afterward people cursed and fled from one another; the manifest love that existed before was transformed into pure murder, slander, blasphemy, condemnation, and persecution, and the resulting great offense far exceeded the good that had been built before.

148. Through this, the **wrath in nature** <term>Wrath in nature: The "dark world" or the first principle of existence, which is a consuming fire that must be tempered by the light of God's love.</term> also ignited and rose up so violently that I and another steadfast brother had to enter into the **process of Christ**, risk our lives, and fight against the dragon-will in them even unto blood: <term>Process of Christ: The spiritual path of the believer mimicking the life, suffering, death, and resurrection of Jesus within their own soul.</term>

149. And if our faithful Helper and Hero <note>Referring to Christ or the Holy Spirit as a champion for the soul.</note> had not himself placed himself so earnestly on the field within us, it would have been impossible for us to stand in this temptation.

150. For the more we infused the dear **Sophia** into them in the spirit, the more haughty they became in their angry dragon-like selfhood; until finally **God** had to intervene and cut down the weeds with a scythe, so that we would not be tempted beyond our ability.

151. This is presented as a **teaching-mirror** <note>Lehrspiegel: An exemplary story or spiritual model used for self-examination and instruction.</note> for our descendants, because the Bridegroom is at the door, and quite wonderful movements in the spirit will reveal themselves; the foolish virgins will love one another as being of their own kind, running together, and will hate and persecute the Bridegroom and the wise virgins. <note>An allusion to the Parable of the Ten Virgins in Matthew 25, where the "wise" are prepared for the coming of Christ and the "foolish" are not.</note>

152. Our own creaturely will (where it sinks itself into **God**, the eternal love, into the innermost ground, stands still in its own willing and working, and gives itself entirely to **God** as...

155. And if it should happen that he knocks at the mind with his rough, fiery rays of poison and hellish thoughts: the spirits are nonetheless immediately stirred, calling and crying out for help, and sending the senses into **God**;

156. Who also rushes to help without delay, so that there is often a great turmoil in the soul until the enemy has been chased away and driven off.

157. 158. Through which the devil mocks God and all good hearts, also carrying out through them in this world what would otherwise be impossible for him.

# The Fifth Chapter.

159. Therefore, let my dear reader be sincerely exhorted to seek only to learn to know himself thoroughly; thus he will certainly find and feel this seven-headed dragon-beast with the whore <note>These figures are drawn from the biblical Book of Revelation (the Beast and the Whore of Babylon), here interpreted as internal psychological states or spiritual corruptions.</note> within himself;

160. With which he must wage war, and may not lay down his weapons nor seek rest in the flesh until it has been cast out from the inner temple or heaven onto the stinking earth of waste and exit; <note>The "inner temple" refers to the sacred space of the soul, while "waste and exit" refers to the physical, decaying world of the senses.</note> then he will have glory before God.

161. The beast and the whore in the outer, coarse world God will also, in His own time, surely cast into the fiery pit.

162. The weapons of the tempter are partly external and physical, partly internal and spiritual.

163. Externally: persecution, imprisonment, bonds, shame, mockery, exile, and death: For he <note>Referring to the devil or the "tempter."</note> has a right to the outer, earthly life; and where we are able to leave it and lay it down, his power is broken.

164. Now, when he has lost this battle and sees that he cannot terrify the mind with his lion's skin <note>A metaphor for overt, aggressive threats or brute force.</note>, he puts on the form of a serpent and lies down by the tree of temptation <note>original: "Versuchbaum." A reference to the Tree of Knowledge in Eden.</note>, presenting the mind with inheritances, wealthy marriages, honor, and great offices.

165. Oh, how holily he knows how to depict everything to us through our reason, and to whistle sweetly, to lure the mind, senses, and imagination, so that it might bite into the beautiful **Venus-apple**! <term>Venus-apple (Venusapfel): A metaphor for worldly or carnal desires and the superficial beauty of earthly life that distracts from spiritual goals.</term>

166. Through this, he has misled and captured many good minds, who thereafter lamented their loss and damage miserably until their end.

## The Sixth Chapter.

### On Prayer.

->I.<-

Because God has opened [the understanding] to me in the light of grace and nature, <note>The "light of grace and nature" refers to a dual source of knowledge common in mystical theology: "grace" being direct revelation from God, and "nature" being the observation of the created world as a mirror of the divine.</note> and also taught me through my own experience, that a godly Christian cannot exist in this mixed world <note>The "mixed world" (German: *vermischte Welt*) refers to the current state of existence where good and evil, light and darkness, are intertwined before the final separation.</note> without resistance,

2. And is not only surrounded on the outside by incredibly many nets and snares of the devil; but also carries within himself his worst enemy <note>The "inner enemy" refers to the self-centered will or "the old Adam" that resists spiritual transformation.</note>, nourishes him, makes him fat and strong, and therefore swims in great dangers like a ship in the sea,

3. Where the devil, the world, God's wrath, and his own flesh and blood constantly pull him into the deep abyss of darkness, and seek to extinguish and swallow up the little divine light kindled in his heart—to which the devil is hostile beyond measure and for which he constantly hungers—or, as it says in Rev. 12, <note>original: "Apoc. 12" refers to the Book of Revelation (the Apocalypse), chapter 12, which describes a dragon attempting to sweep away a woman and her child with a flood of water.</note> wishes to drown it;

### On Prayer.

4. Thus, I could do nothing better than to cast my own will forever into **God's** most beloved will, and with pleading and sighing, unceasingly persist for the governance of His **Holy Spirit**:

5. I also undertook not the slightest thing without prayer and the consent of my dear **Guide**, <note>The "Guide" (German: *Führer*) likely refers to the internal direction of the Holy Spirit or the "Inner Word," a common concept in mystical devotion where the believer seeks direct divine permission for all actions.</note> because I had to walk a completely untrodden path and enter into **Christ's** poor life, which is nonetheless incomprehensible and contrary to all reason.

6. In this, I had to expect much offense from all natural men; <term>natural men: a term used in 17th-century divinity to describe people living by human reason and instinct alone, without spiritual rebirth or enlightenment.</term> friends and enemies alike would rise up against me and regard, hate, and abandon me as a peculiar newcomer who wanted to bring forth a completely strange doctrine and a path impossible and untrodden for human nature; in this, my thoughts did not deceive me.

7. And although I intended to escape the storm and bury myself in silence to avoid it; yet **God** directed my intention quite differently, and with cunning and love drew me right into the battle, from which I wanted to flee like Jonah. <note>original: "mit Jona entfliehen." A reference to the biblical Prophet Jonah who tried to run away from God's command to preach to Nineveh, only to be brought back by a great fish.</note>

8. Because I have now learned from this that my will and **God's** will were in conflict, and He yet became too strong for me, I finally had to surrender my will completely and let **God** rule as He pleased; even though my reason could not understand or foresee where **God** was aiming.

9. Until finally, through His great mercy, after the time had been endured, I landed in a safe harbor where my soul finds rest, and the faith-

11. And I am highly rejoiced, and heartily thank the Most High at all times, who has graciously preserved for me one constant, faithful fellow-brother among so many,

12. 13. Now, the Holy Spirit and one's own need are the best teachers of prayer; and we would have no need for prayer books and formulas if we were all of one mind and had the Holy Spirit of prayer within us.

14. But because there are many heads and many minds—one seeking and finding **God** high above the stars, another in the air, and the fewest within themselves—everyone goes their own way and prays according to how they are spiritually aligned. <term>spiritually aligned: the original German "constelliret" (constellated) refers to a person's inner spiritual makeup or "internal stars," which determines their character and how they relate to the Divine.</term>

15. A beastly man <term>beastly man: a person living purely by physical instincts and animal nature, lacking spiritual enlightenment.</term> reaches no further than into the atmospheric heavens, namely into the *Soul of the World*, <note>original: "Animam Mundi." In the philosophy of the time, this was the life force that animated the physical earth and elements.</note> and into the elements.

16. Others, who go somewhat deeper, penetrate into the *Spirit of the World*, <note>original: "Spiritum Mundi." This was considered a higher, celestial spirit that permeated the stars and the sun, acting as a bridge between the physical and spiritual realms.</note> or into the starry heavens as far as the sun; they are forbidden from pressing any further.

17. A diabolical man penetrates into the dark world; for his magic seeks only to bring forth works and words of darkness, according to the lust of his flesh and his evil will.

Verse 15 was interrupted, and the text now transitions to describing the spiritual process of the "reborn" individual.</meta>

<page-num>121</page-num>
<header>On Prayer.</header>

18. But the reborn person enters into themselves with their spiritual power <note>original: "Magie," referring here to the innate creative power of the soul's will to manifest spiritual reality</note>, into the true holy heaven of the holy light-tincture <term>light-tincture: a mystical substance or quality representing the pure, life-giving essence of divine light</term>, even before the holy Trinity; they grasp the self-existent, speaking Word or Sophia <note>Sophia: the personification of Divine Wisdom, often viewed in this tradition as the bride of the soul</note> into their desire,

19. And with the **creative Word** <note>original: "Verbo Fiat," the Latin command "Let there be" used by God in creation</note>, they bring forth the holy Trinity with heavenly wisdom in all their prayers:

20. And such a person alone worships the right, true, triune **God** in spirit and truth, and their prayer is "Yes" and "Amen" in heaven and on earth.

21. Others cling to their usual words and create forms based on their own will, according to the desires of the outer life, in which there is no real power.

22. I have indeed heard many otherwise well-meaning people speak of their conduct, claiming that they can serve **God** and worship Him in all their outward works.

23. I do not wish to contradict this, because not everyone knows how to distinguish between **God** and nature, or the **levels** <note>original: "Gradus," referring to the different stages or degrees of existence</note> of the worlds or heavens, nor do they understand what it means to pray rightly in spirit and truth.

24. Therefore, for the sake of the lack of understanding in the simple-hearted, one must have compassion and look to **Christ**, who is our advocate with **God** our heavenly Father, and who has mercy on the blind sheep who have no true teacher or guide.

25. Among the faithful, there is still a great difference, depending on whether each person is still stable or unstable <term>stable or unstable: the original "fix" and "unfix" are alchemical terms describing whether a substance is solid and unchanging or volatile; here applied to spiritual constancy</term> in true love, or whether they stand in shallow or deep knowledge; to elaborate on this is unnecessary:

<sig>H 5</sig>
<meta>catchword: GOtt (God)</meta>

<summary>This page defines "true prayer" as an internal act of the reborn soul, using spiritual power to connect with Divine Wisdom (Sophia). It distinguishes between this empowered prayer and the hollow, formal prayers of those focused on the external world, while advocating for compassion toward the "simple-hearted."</summary>
<keywords>rebirth, spiritual power, light-tincture, Sophia, creative word, triune God, spiritual degrees, Christ as advocate, alchemical constancy, inner prayer</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the exploration of true prayer, moving from the theoretical distinction between internal and external worship to the author's personal, mystical practice of reaching the Divine.</meta>

<page-num>122</page-num>
### The Sixth Chapter.

God is and remains a giver of Himself, if we only desire Him with earnestness.

26. Right praying, according to my experience and **practice** <note>original: "Praxi"</note>, is not to make many words; but rather a sinking of the soul-spirit <term>soul-spirit: the innermost essence of the soul that acts as the vehicle for the will's ascent</term> or will into God, and a bringing-forth of the holy Trinity and Wisdom through the seven forms of nature <term>seven forms of nature: the fundamental qualities or "fountains" through which the Divine manifests from nothingness into being</term>.

27. This occurs through magic or the desire of faith, as the will takes hold of itself in a magic <note>original: "Magie," here meaning the creative, formative power of the soul's concentrated desire</note> and desire for that which the will wishes to have and ask for.

28. But because the will is very thin, like a thought, and must have a body in which it can act, it takes Christ’s promises into its **imagination** as its body, and penetrates with desire into God;

29. Yet through this desire, it makes itself hard, harsh, dark, and anxious, and feels within itself nothing but doubt and "No," which initially **disturbed** <note>original: "turbiret"</note> me no little before I understood the birth of God.

30. For I did not want "No," but "Yes," and yet found only more anxiety the more strongly I ignited myself in the desire, so that I even had to withdraw; but after a short while I returned, cursed the "No" and the doubt, and took hold of a desire to penetrate into God's light.

31. Then, in the penetrating desire, the Spirit of God met me and, in the **union** <note>original: "Conjunction"</note>, struck up the fire, whereby my anxious will felt itself sensibly in a lovely joy within a light, and received its answer.

32. For then everything was "Yes" and "Amen," and no doubt was to be felt.

<meta>catchword: 33.</meta>

<summary>The author describes the internal, "magical" process of prayer, wherein the soul's will struggles through doubt and darkness to achieve a fiery union with the Spirit of God. This transformative experience moves the seeker from an anxious "No" to a divine "Yes and Amen."</summary>
<keywords>prayer, soul-spirit, seven forms of nature, magic of faith, spiritual imagination, doubt, divine union, light-fire</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author’s personal account of his spiritual exercises, specifically how his experiences in prayer allowed him to finally understand the difficult mystical writings of Jacob Böhme regarding the nature of God’s wrath and love.</meta>

<page-num>123</page-num>
<header>Of Prayer.</header>
<meta>catchword: 41.</meta>

33. Through this practice <note>original: "Praxis"</note>, into which God led me in prayer, the foundation of Böhme’s **seven forms** and **three principles**—of which he writes in all his works—became open to me.

34. From this I have seen so much: that his readers, unless they are personally led by God into this practice, will find it difficult to grasp the depth of his meaning.

35. Because I have experienced that passing through the strict wrath of God—the first dark principle <term>first dark principle: in the theology of Jacob Böhme, this represents the harsh, fiery, and "dark" root of nature that must be transformed by light into love</term>—is a rigorous path, which requires not only earnestness but also an undaunted courage, which nevertheless does not lie within a human's own power;

36. Therefore, let the loving follower be informed by experience to hold love firmly in his desire and **imagination** <note>original: "Imagination," referring to the soul's power to form internal images and focus the will</note>, and by no means let it go while under the weight of the burden;

37. Thus he will always find courage when the wrath terrifies him or introduces doubt and unbelief; let him simply curse those doubts with confidence and immediately contemplate <note>original: "speculire," meaning to direct the inner vision or focus the mind intensely</note> love with his imagination; then he will surely feel how the wrath retreats and falls away.

38. In the beginning, I had to endure a hard blow, as the wrath thrust a "sin against the Holy Spirit" into my mind-spirit <term>mind-spirit: "Gemüth," the inner seat of thoughts, emotions, and the will</term>, making it seem as if I had cursed God:

39. And because of this, I fell into a distorted humility, for I had not yet read Böhme and gained an understanding from him of what cursing, love, and wrath, or what "God" and "not-God" truly were.

40. To an unenlightened mind, our description will indeed seem strange, and even to a half-enlightened one who is not yet established in such exercise, it may seem somewhat questionable:

<summary>The author explains how his internal struggles in prayer mirrored Jacob Böhme’s mystical theories, specifically the transition from the "dark principle" of God's wrath to the "light" of His love. He advises practitioners to focus their imagination on Love to overcome spiritual terror and doubt.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Böhme, seven forms of nature, three principles, wrath of God, imagination, spiritual practice, Gemüth, mystical prayer, dark principle</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of spiritual practice and the internal struggle for enlightenment, transitioning from the author’s personal account of overcoming doubt to a universal parable of spiritual growth based on Jacob Böhme’s theology.</meta>

# The Sixth Chapter.

<p align="center">->41.<-</p>

41. I present to you a parable <note>original: "Gleichniß"</note> of a seed: See, when it is sown into its mother <note>referring to the earth or the nurturing matrix of nature</note>, it must die and pass through all **seven forms of nature** <term>seven forms of nature: Jacob Böhme’s core system describing the stages of cosmic and spiritual manifestation, moving from harsh contraction and friction to light and expression</term> before it brings fruit again; no one who loves the truth in any measure can deny this.

42. For the growing power in the seed drives forward through the help of rain and sunshine in the desire to grow; for if there were no **magnetic desire** <term>magnetic desire: the attractive force within the soul or nature that "draws" spirit into substance</term> in the seed, the seed would be deaf or dead and nothing could grow.

43. In the same way, the magical or magnetic desire of the soulful will or mind-spirit <note>original: "Gemüth," the seat of deep emotion and thought</note> is the creator and bringer-forth of that which the will has grasped in its imagination—namely, the noble, gentle light of **GOD**.

44. When you have thus brought forth within you the beautiful, gentle light, in which the Holy Trinity dwells with the Eternal Wisdom, then indeed your whole body is light, in which you have great joy.

45. But it does not last long in the outer mind-spirit; for the **red dragon** <note>a biblical and Böhmean symbol for the egoistic, fallen nature of the soul that seeks to dominate</note>, acting as the soulful worm or the dark **principle** <note>original: "Principium," referring to the root of divine wrath and darkness in nature</note>, hungers fiercely for it and swallows it down again into its great magnetic hunger of fury <note>original: "Grimmenhunger," a hunger characterized by wrath or intense bitterness</note>, so as to extinguish it.

46. If you now wish to have your noble light shining within you again, you must simply bring it forth in yourself once more; so that in your soul there is a fierce struggle between love and wrath for the upper hand <note>original: "Oberregiment," meaning the ruling power or dominion</note>.

47. This drives you constantly into prayer and allows little idle time; for it requires a very great earnestness.

<summary>The author uses the parable of a germinating seed to explain how the light of God is birthed within the human soul through a recurring struggle against the "dark principle" of wrath.</summary>
<keywords>parable of the seed, seven forms of nature, magnetic desire, mind-spirit, red dragon, spiritual struggle, Jacob Böhme, inner light</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the transition from the internal spiritual struggle described in the previous chapter to the practical "priesthood" of the believer. # Of Prayer.

48. This struggle lasts within you until finally wrath is exhausted and must surrender itself to love; from this, the great mercy of GOD is born in the soul.

49. I write from my own experience, and I leave every person's own experience blameless; although I have had to suffer much for it and lie still under the dark, black blanket of the devil, because the dragon has risen up within my brothers and has always sought to drown my noble **little pearl of light** <note>original: "Lichtperlein," a term used by Jacob Böhme to describe the fragile but precious spark of divine wisdom or the "Pearl of Great Price" within the soul</note> with his stream of fury;

50. But through the earnest help of my faithful **virgins** <note>likely referring to the "Virgin Sophia" or Divine Wisdom, a central figure in this school of mysticism representing the celestial counterpart to the soul</note>, he could not break through to victory. Eternal thanks be to God!

51. Now, when you have been readmitted to the Christian state by Christ and have been reborn through water and Spirit, you are a chosen priest of the Most High, to whom the right is given to enter into the **Holy of Holies** <note>referring to the innermost sanctuary of the Tabernacle, here used as a metaphor for the deepest part of the human spirit</note> within yourself.

52. For the Word is now revealed **essentially** <term>essentialiter: meaning "in substance" or "in essence"; the Word is not just a concept but a living presence within the believer</term> in your heart and mouth, and the divine censer—namely, the spirit of prayer—has been entrusted to you,

53. So that you, as a true member of Christ, should offer prayers, intercessions, praise, thanks, and sacrifices of atonement for all people—for Jews, Turks, and Heathens <note>the author uses the standard 17th-century categories for non-Christians: Jews, Muslims ("Turks"), and practitioners of other religions ("Heathens")</note> who still stand outside in the outer court of the flesh—and you should present your soul for theirs in Christ Jesus as an **offering** <term>Anathema: here used in the sense of a "votive offering" or a self-sacrifice, where the believer is willing to be "set apart" for the sake of others' salvation</term>,

54. You must constantly wrap yourself in the love of Jesus and introduce this love, through prayer, into the **wrath** <note>the "wrath" refers to the "dark fire" or the harsh, egoistic principle within nature and the soul</note> in its fiery, sharp hunger, so as to soothe and extinguish it.

55. You must not [bury] your spiritual pound in your

<meta>This sentence breaks off and continues on the next page with the word "own."</meta>

<summary>The author describes the spiritual transition from internal conflict to a state of "priesthood," where the reborn soul uses prayer to transform divine wrath into love. He emphasizes that the believer has a duty to intercede for all humanity, acting as a mediator through the indwelling "Word."</summary>

# The Sixth Chapter.

[...not to be] buried in your own soul, nor provide heavenly stores for yourself alone, but you must always let your little stream flow out through prayer into all your **fellow branches** <note>referring to fellow believers as branches on the same vine of Christ</note>, and bring to God your fruits of love and firstlings; thus God can bless you and continually impart new strengths.

56. And through such practice, you will progress further into the **interior** <term>Inneren: the inner spiritual life or the "inner man"</term> in one year than otherwise in many years; a fact that all who are experienced in these matters must confess along with me.

57. You must not go out—but only go in, and let God’s Spirit go out; He will rightly perform your prayers and win over lovely brides, who will heartily thank both you and God that salvation has come to their souls through your diligent work.

58. And though it may happen that their **selfhood** <term>Eigenheit: the stubborn, ego-driven nature of the unregenerate soul</term>, acting as the **red dragon** <note>a biblical image from Revelation, here representing the fierce, wrathful resistance of the ego against divine love</note> within them, will not surrender to love and seeks to oppose you with animalistic zeal and wrath;

59. Then you must simply remain in the love of JESUS and never let them out of your **imagination** <term>Imagination: in this mystical tradition, it refers to the focused creative power of the soul's will</term> and will; hold your angry brothers fast **by the heels** <note>an allusion to Jacob holding Esau’s heel at birth, signifying a spiritual struggle for the birthright or transformation</note>, and with your love in prayer and spirit, penetrate into their wrath until it surrenders to the love of JESUS and is transformed into gentle mercy.

60. For you yourself, together with your JESUS, must **birth love out of the wrath** <note>original: "die Liebe in dem Zorn ausgebären"; a central concept in the mysticism of Jacob Böhme, where the light of love is born out of the friction of the soul's "fire" or wrathful nature</note>, and birth your angry brothers anew into Christ Jesus, as into eternal love,

61. And consider only that you have been anointed by God for this purpose, and placed at your brothers’ **press** <note>original: "Presse"; referring to the "winepress" of suffering or spiritual labor where the "grapes" of the soul are crushed to produce the "wine" of divine love</note>.

to pray for them, and to reconcile them with God, to wed them, and to bind them.

62. And that such should be your office in this **earthly tabernacle** <note>a biblical metaphor for the human body and mortal life</note>, wherein you must be faithful until the end, and must glorify and exalt JESUS above all power, might, strength, and kingdoms.

63. And even if the self-will in one person or another opposes this; simply stand still in your will, and see where the **Angel of Counsel** <note>a title for Christ, derived from the Greek Septuagint translation of Isaiah 9:6</note> will direct His message.

66. 67. And he should by no means spend his time in idleness and laziness, as the world wrongly judges him, nor desire to fatten himself on the abundance of the rich.

# The Sixth Chapter.

...[to feast], to practice usury, to scrape and hoard, to buy and sell, to run and rush about, and to live according to the world’s mind, so that he might have rest in the world, and peace, honor, and wealth in the flesh;

69. And would not have to leave the world, follow Christ in **rebirth** <term>Wiedergeburt: the spiritual transformation or "born again" experience central to pietist theology</term>, and renounce all temporal things, down to the very shirt used to cover his shame.

70. 71. The devil himself also covers them with his dark cloak of vice, so that they may not be recognized in this world, lest they rob souls from his **pigpen** <note>original: "Saupferch," a disparaging metaphor for the domain of sin where the devil keeps souls like livestock</note>.

72. And certainly the world is not worthy of such faithful souls; therefore GOD lets them remain so hidden, that they may not be recognized and torn apart by the devil's **fattened swine** <note>original: "Mastschweinen," referring to those who have grown "fat" or arrogant on worldly pleasures and sins</note>.

73. It is also a very great grace when GOD deems a rich man's **unrighteous mammon** <term>Mammon: a biblical term for wealth, often personified as a false god or a corrupting influence</term> worthy to be helpful and refreshing to such hidden poor ones:

74. Since it is GOD’S Spirit alone that works this, because they cannot be recognized outwardly, nor do they burden anyone or complain of their need, but are content with GOD and how He deals with them.

75. For they are His poor, and not the world’s poor; therefore GOD knows them well, and they also know GOD, and seek to honor Him and worship Him alone until their end.

### I.

Firstly and above all else, it is to be known briefly—and only as much as is required here regarding the divine foundation (for the matter can be found and read more extensively and in greater detail elsewhere, specifically in the writings of the blessed man of God, Jacob Böhme <note>Jacob Böhme (1575–1624) was a German mystic whose complex cosmological and theological ideas deeply influenced Western esotericism.</note>, and in the <term>Theosophical Epistles</term> <note>Theosophy here refers to "divine wisdom," a tradition of mystical speculation about the nature of God and the world.</note> of the highly enlightened and blessed Gichtel <note>Johann Georg Gichtel (1638–1710), a prominent follower of Böhme who edited his works and founded the "Angelic Brothers" or "Gichtelians."</note>)—that the first man, Adam, was created in the image of God with all divine powers and properties. This was so that he, with the assistance of his granted heavenly Virgin Sophia <note>Sophia is the personified "Divine Wisdom." In this tradition, she is seen as Adam's celestial bride or the spiritual mirror of God.</note>, could have given birth to humans of his own kind through the <term>Magic</term> <note>original: "Magiam"; in this context, it refers to the spiritual, creative power of the will or spirit to manifest form.</note> of the spirit. Thus, he could have gradually filled this <term>Place</term> <note>original: "Locum"; referring to the physical and spiritual domain of the world.</note> of the world with God-men or Angel-men, in place of the fallen spirits who had occupied it and inhabited it in the light before their fall, had he remained faithful to his heavenly Virgin. At that time it was said: *And God saw everything He had made, and behold, it was very good.* Genesis 1:31.

2. However, after the created animals and birds were presented to Adam so that he might give them names according to their properties and qualities, and he saw how male and female with <note>The text breaks off here; the catchword "einan-" suggests the next word is "einander," meaning "with one another."</note>

...cajoled one another and played in love, Satan infected his mind so that he also looked around for an external woman and lusted after one who would also be around him, and with whom he could also enjoy himself externally.

<margin>Genesis 2:18</margin>
3. His heavenly Virgin <note>In the theosophy of Jacob Böhme, the "Virgin Sophia" represents Divine Wisdom and was Adam's original spiritual bride before the creation of Eve.</note>, seeing this unfaithfulness of his—that he had left her—departed from him. Thus, he became powerless to bring forth offspring from within himself. Therefore God said: It is not good that man should be alone; I will make him a helper to be around him (so that God’s purpose might stand: to fill this place <note>original: "Locum"; referring to the spatial realm or station of the world in the cosmic hierarchy</note> of the world in stead of the fallen spirits) and took one of his ribs, and built an external woman from it, and thus separated the masculine and feminine tincture <note>original: "Tinctur"; a technical term in alchemy and mysticism referring to a spiritual life-force or essential quality that colors and gives life to matter</note> from one another.

4. In this state, they still retained the somewhat weakened image of God and their paradisiacal body, which was formed from the quintessence <note>original: "quinta Essentia"; the purest, most essential substance of the heavens or the paradisiacal earth, distinct from the four gross elements</note> of the paradisiacal earth. In this body, they should still have been able to give birth to their likeness from themselves in a paradisiacal, heavenly manner, despite the separation of the tinctures <note>original: "Tincturen"</note>. They could have reunited these tinctures in their prayers in Christ—the spoken Word of God and the Re-generator—if they had, by their own will, penetrated out of themselves and out of nature into God's will. It was for this reason that God tested them with the tree of temptation <note>The "Versuchbaum" or Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil</note>, to see whether they would use their will to rise above themselves, moving out of the fire and into the light, where the flower of the heavenly tincture <note>original: "Tinctur"</note> stands in Jesus,

...will to grasp, so that she stretched out her hand and plucked the fruit from the **Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil**, and ate of it, and she also gave some to Adam, so that he likewise ate. In an instant, the divine light was extinguished in both of them—the light in which their image stood in true righteousness and holiness (**Ephesians 4:24**). And they saw that their paradisiacal, brightly shining <term>power-body</term> <note>original: "Kraftleib"; in the theosophical tradition of Jacob Böhme, this refers to the spiritual, luminous body humans possessed before the Fall.</note> was transformed into a coarse, dark <term>animal-body</term> <note>original: "Thierleib"; the mortal, physical body of flesh and bone.</note> with beastly <term>procreative organs</term> <note>original: "Geburtsglieder"; literally "birth-members," referring to the sexual organs which the author views as a consequence of the Fall.</note>. Then they realized that they had been deceived by **Satan**, who begrudged them their blissful state in Paradise and had thus lured them out with malicious cunning. They had to feel ashamed before one another of the procreative organs they had acquired and make themselves aprons—just as we still have to do today, to cover ourselves before the face of the holy angels. And it is because of this deformity that our animal body must die and must decay in the grave.

6. ...acted weakly, and instead of bearing **God** (namely the divine image) out of themselves—as Eve also believed she had received the <term>man Jehovah</term> <note>original: "den Mann Jehovah"; a reference to the German translation of Genesis 4:1, where Eve exclaims she has gotten a man from the Lord, 혹은 "the man, the Lord."</note>, **Genesis 4:1**—Eve instead bore the spirit of the world, ignited by **Satan**, from which **Cain** was born. He was the child of the grasping of the self-will, who had **Satan** within him.

With the birth of **Abel** they were more fortunate; although they themselves did not know what kind of spirit was in him. Therefore, they gave him a name meaning "Vanity" or "Nothing," <note>The Hebrew name *Habel* (Abel) literally means "breath" or "vapor," implying something transitory or insignificant.</note> as if he were a wretched one, and in nature he was indeed as a "nothing." And such we must also become if **God’s** Spirit is to dwell in us. 

Such children are very rare, who are baptized by the **Holy Spirit** in the mother’s womb; one finds among thousands perhaps not even one, unless **God** reveals Himself in the fulfillment of His <term>Covenant of Grace</term> <note>original: "Gnadenbund"; the theological concept of God's promise of salvation to humanity.</note> within them, as occurred with **John the Baptist** in the New Testament, who was to be a forerunner of **Jesus**. These children are conformed to the likeness of **Jesus**, whom **God** baptizes with heavenly fire, so that nature becomes a hell to them; therefore they live not according to the flesh, but according to the spirit. This is why **Abel** took no wife, just as **John** also did not.

Indeed, he also sincerely admonishes the Thessalonians toward sanctification, so that each one may know how to keep his vessel (or his wife) <note>The "vessel" is a biblical metaphor often interpreted as either one's own body or one's spouse.</note> in sanctification and honor, and not in the passion of lust like the heathens who know nothing of **GOD**; for **GOD** has not called them to impurity, but to sanctification, **1 Thessalonians 4:3, 7**. So he also admonishes the Romans that they should walk honorably, not in lewdness (chambering) <note>German: *Cammern*. This refers to "chambering," an old term for illicit or excessive sexual indulgence, often cited alongside "wantonness."</note> and unchastity, but rather put on the **Lord JESUS Christ** and care for the body in such a way that it does not become lewd, **Romans 13:13, 14**.

Likewise, the holy Apostle **Peter** also admonishes that one should abstain from fleshly lusts, which war against the soul, **1 Peter 2:11**, and that from now on one should live no longer for the lusts of men but for the will of **GOD**, **2 Peter 4:2** <note>This likely refers to 1 Peter 4:2, as the second epistle of Peter only has three chapters.</note>, and should flee the perishable lusts of the world so that one may again become a partaker of the divine nature, **2 Peter 1:4**; and that those who walk after the flesh in unclean lust are kept for the day of judgment for punishment, **2 Peter 2:9, 10**. So also says the holy **John**: everything that is in the world—namely, the lust of the flesh, the lust of the eyes, and a prideful life—is not from the Father, but from the world, **1 John 2:16**.

Furthermore, we find in the history of **Tobit** <note>The Book of Tobit is part of the Apocrypha. It was widely read in this period for its teachings on family life and marriage.</note> that the devil has power over those who take wives for the sake of unchastity, like dumb cattle, **Tobit 6:17, 18**; and therefore the evil spirit **Asmodeus** <note>A demon often associated with lust who, in the Book of Tobit, kills the suitors of Sarah before their marriages can be consummated.</note> killed seven of them who wished to lie with **Sarah**, **Raguel's** daughter, **Tobit 3:7, 8**. many other...

for the sake of their unchastity, as he himself states in verse 5, [he] yielded somewhat and thus <term>condescended (condescendiret)</term> <note>In this context, "condescended" means to waive a right or lower a standard to meet someone at their current level of spiritual maturity.</note> for a time, because he could not yet speak to them as spiritual people, but only as fleshly ones (1 Corinthians 3:1). # The Bookbinder

is to paste in the figures in the following manner:

1) The two standing side-by-side with the heading: **Practical Theosophy** <note>original: *Theosophia Practica*. This likely refers to the collected works of Johann Georg Gichtel or a similar Böhmean mystic, where "Practical Theosophy" refers to the lived experience of divine wisdom.</note>, are to be attached to a small <term>stub</term> <note>A narrow strip of paper or parchment, called a *Fälßchen* in the original, bound into the spine to allow an illustration to be glued in so that it opens flat.</note> before the title page.

2) The **Wheel of Birth** <note>A central symbol in theosophical mysticism, particularly in the works of Jakob Böhme, representing the eternal cycle of nature and the seven "source-spirits" or qualities of existence.</note> is to be placed on a stub at page 1, before the first chapter.

3) The remaining two **copperplates** <note>Illustrations printed from etched or engraved copper sheets, a standard method for high-quality images in the 17th and 18th centuries.</note> however, belong to pages 14 and 47, before the second and third chapters.

**NB.** <note>Abbreviation for the Latin *Nota Bene*, meaning "Note well."</note> The book must not be trimmed too much <note>referring to the process of "ploughing" the edges of the paper to make them even</note>, as the wide margin should remain everywhere. <note>In this period, wide margins were highly valued, both as a sign of a high-quality "large paper" edition and to provide space for the reader to add their own handwritten notes or "marginalia."</note>

<image-desc>A decorative typographic ornament featuring a central horizontal bar flanked by symmetrical scrollwork and leaf motifs.</image-desc>

<summary>This page provides specific technical instructions for the bookbinder regarding the placement of four mystical illustrations, including the "Wheel of Birth." It emphasizes the preservation of wide margins, likely to allow for reader annotations.</summary>
